decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:1:# DR-2026-05-24 — Best-Practice Supersession Protocol
decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:4:**Authored:** 2026-05-25 (session_2026-05-23-bpc-rewrite-phase-b-closure)
decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:12:PI standing rule #6 (canonical workplan) and mission doctrinal commitments 2, 3, and 5 hold that best practice for the guidebook is determined by the evidence hierarchy, not by code consensus or by whatever evidence happened to be in the corpus when a slug was first scoped. The Phase B citation-mining protocol (skill: `citation-miner`) surfaces references that cite or are cited by an anchor source — a citation-graph operation. It does **not** check whether each anchor source remains the best current evidence for the parameter × population × outcome it supports.
decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:14:A slug closed under the old citation_mining_complete=1 bar can have all anchor sources protocol-mined and still rest on stale best evidence — for example, citing the 2010 ANSI/ASA S12.60 revision when a 2024 revision exists, or citing 2018-era systematic reviews when 2024 Cochrane or JBI work supersedes the synthesis. That is exactly the failure mode mission doctrinal commitment 2 prohibits ("a best-practice claim derived solely from code consensus is in error" — and by symmetric reasoning, a best-practice claim derived from a *superseded* Tier-3 source is also in error, because the operative evidence hierarchy includes a temporal dimension).
decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:59:2. **Semiannual sweep** — Across the full corpus, every six months, run a temporal-pass supersession check: for every anchor source whose last_checked_date is older than the sweep horizon, re-run the supersession search filtered to publication date > last_checked_date. Sweep dates: 2026-12-01 (first post-DR sweep, ~6 months after this DR) and every 6 months thereafter. The sweep cadence is itself a methodological declaration — between sweeps, the BPC's evidence base is correct as of the last sweep, not as of "today."
decisions/DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol.md:85:3. **Grounds best practice in evidence hierarchy?** Yes — supersession searches are tier-prioritized; a Tier-1 candidate supersedes a Tier-3 anchor regardless of recency, and within the same tier, recency tie-breaks.

decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-architecture-unification.md:14:Maximum-effort review (this session) found the question ~80% answered by existing doctrine — decision D-D (`evidence-methodology.md` §§1.4–1.7), the role layer (`armature_v4.md` §4.5), the audience structure (`audience-priority.md`), and the weighting-profile design (`workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §5) — but the answer was (a) scattered across six-plus documents with no unifying statement, (b) obscured by a vocabulary collision in which "Tier 1/Tier 2" name design modes in `mission-and-epistemics.md` commitment 4 and evidence tiers everywhere else, plus a third "Mode P/Mode S" vocabulary, (c) un-ratified (four keystone DRs PROPOSED), and (d) unbuilt (`evidence_cell_state` = 0 rows). decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-architecture-unification.md:22:**Problem.** `evidence-methodology.md` §2.3 allows a cell to reach `provisional` on T4–6 convergence alone, and §3.4 supplies rendering language for it, while `tier-system.md` §3 and `schemas/directness.py` (NON-ANCHORING) forbid exactly that stratum from anchoring best-practice claims. The `code_floor_only` flag (migration 026) covers only the T6-only case, so a T4/T5-only cell passes `v_best_practice`; the policymaker weighting profile up-weights exactly this stratum. This is the mechanism by which a policymaker rendering could launder code-convergence into best practice.
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-architecture-unification.md:24:**Recommendation.** (a) **Scale-tagging:** every determination is tagged with the design scale its claim speaks to; T4–6-only determinations are Universal-Mode regulatory determinations, never rendered as best practice in any register. (b) **Flag extension:** `regulatory_stratum_only` (T4–6-only) supersedes/extends `code_floor_only` (T6-only); `v_best_practice` excludes it. (c) **Re-graining rule:** a T4/T5 source escapes GRAIN_CODE (→ GRAIN_AGGREGATE) only when its own evidence basis is documented traceable to T1/T2, provenance recorded — operationalizing the "if the standard itself rests on T1/T2 evidence" clause already in `tier-system.md` §1.
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-architecture-unification.md:52:**Recommendation.** Ratify advocacy-brief as a use-pattern within the disabled-person primary audience (extendable to carers, DPO staff): emphasis = code-vs-best-practice delta + rights framing + citable evidence strength. Amends `audience-priority.md` (use-pattern table) post-ratification; no change to the four-audience structure or priority rules.

decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:11:Two incompatible schemas exist for `evidence_cell_state`, the empty table meant to hold the project's central per-cell best-practice determination:
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:15:**B. `workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §4 (2026-06-22; explicitly "sketch; finalize as migration 026," never built):** keyed on `(slug, population, jurisdiction)`. Convergence is a flat `convergence` enum column with no directness distinction. Adds real, valuable capability schema A lacks: `governing_refs` (anti-hallucination), `rule_version` + `derivation_sha` (staleness detection), `tier_basis`, `code_floor_only`, `value_range`, and a `falsification` condition.
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:22:2. **Do not add a `jurisdiction` dimension to this table at all.** `governance/tier-system.md`'s corridor-width worked example is explicit that "best practice" (evidence-anchored, e.g. 2440mm) and "regulatory floor" (jurisdiction-specific code convergence, e.g. 1800mm) are different *kinds* of claim, and that treating jurisdiction-convergence as best-practice evidence is the named failure mode this doctrine exists to prevent. Baking jurisdiction into `evidence_cell_state`'s identity — even as a nullable/sentinel column — puts a jurisdiction-specific dimension inside a table whose determination is supposed to be jurisdiction-agnostic by definition. Instead:
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:25:5. **Add schema B's provenance/reproducibility columns**: `tier_basis`, `governing_refs` (JSON array, `CHECK (... json_valid(...))`), `rule_version`, `derivation_sha`, `code_floor_only`, `falsification_condition`. Replace B's unstructured `value_range TEXT` with **structured** `value_min REAL, value_max REAL, value_unit TEXT` — matching the numeric/unit convention `jurisdictional_values` (and `source_value_extractions`) already use, so a code-floor-vs-best-practice delta is a plain numeric comparison rather than string parsing.
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:29:9. **Add the derived views**, with one correction to B's own sketch: `v_best_practice` must exclude `code_floor_only = 1` rows. `best-practices-assessment-system.md` §3's own hard rule states a Tier-6-only cell is `provisional`/`pending`, never the evidence basis for "best practice" — so a view that filters `state IN ('stated','provisional')` without also excluding `code_floor_only=1` would surface exactly the jurisdiction-code-as-best-practice conflation tier-system.md names as the failure mode this whole schema exists to prevent. `v_code_floor_only` is implemented as a join against the new `jurisdictional_values` table (not a bare self-filter), so the code-floor-delta surface `best-practices-assessment-system.md` §4 Phase 4 promises is actually computable from structured columns.
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md:37:- `workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §4's SQL sketch is superseded by the actual migration; its phasing plan (§8) is otherwise unaffected.

decisions/DR-2026-07-12-tier3-stated-threshold.md:11:`decisions/DR-2026-05-29-evidence-hierarchy-reconciliation.md` redefined Tier 3 as "rarely the sole basis" for best-practice claims (now codified in `governance/tier-system.md` line 19: *"Supporting evidence for best-practice claims; rarely the sole basis"*), while flagging, explicitly unresolved:
decisions/DR-2026-07-12-tier3-stated-threshold.md:21:1. decisions/DR-2026-07-13-value-genealogy-and-derivation-handshake.md:4:- Date: 2026-07-13 (authored in the evidence-architecture unification session, on the owner's directives: adjudicate best-practice-vs-consensus per mode; handle swept-path vs turning-radius, device-class heterogeneity, and multimodal width; fan out per population; decide whether a bottom-up/top-down handshake is required; harness the scattered md/yaml/json evidence body)
decisions/DR-2026-07-13-value-genealogy-and-derivation-handshake.md:6:- Affects (if ratified): `scripts/migrations/` (one follow-on migration), `workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` Phase 1 (extraction pass gains genealogy fields), `scripts/assess/assess_cell.py` (post-ratification: derivation-path gates), `references/fdr/` (naming + merge path), `governance/evidence-architecture.md` (gains §4.5 genealogy + §5.5 handshake on ratification)
decisions/DR-2026-07-13-value-genealogy-and-derivation-handshake.md:11:**Problem 1 (genealogy).** The project's central adjudication — best practice (dignity-bearing, most accommodating) vs consensus/precedent — currently has no computable form. The corridor-width genealogy proves the stakes: 1500mm turning "circles" in 24 jurisdictions trace to one 1970s anthropometric root (REF-00059/60: "based on research conducted in the 1970s… need to be updated"); the 1800mm passing consensus has **no traceable root anywhere in the corpus**; while 2440/2400mm has **two independent measurement/participatory roots** (ASL proxemics Co-1; swept-path anthropometry T3 entire-sample) plus an untraced regulatory convergence (MEXT T5 — a `committee_assertion` under this DR's own vocabulary until its derivation is mined). Precedent counts documents; evidence counts roots — and today nothing in the data layer can count roots. Worse, values are being compared across incommensurable **measurement paradigms** (static turning circle vs dynamic swept-path envelope — 97% of turns are asymmetric spin turns; scooters have no pivot at all) and across **device classes** (multiple codes self-declare their figures manual-wheelchair-only).
decisions/DR-2026-07-13-value-genealogy-and-derivation-handshake.md:21:Extend the (empty) B6 substrate with: `root_id` (shared identifier for one evidentiary root), `root_type` CHECK (`measurement_primary` / `participatory_finding` / `committee_assertion` / `derived_calculation` / `untraced`), `root_ref_id` (FK to `evidence_sources` when the root document is in corpus), `echo_of` (transmission-chain note), `measurement_paradigm` CHECK (`swept_path_dynamic` / `static_turning_circle` / `static_clearance` / `anthropometric_percentile` / `instrumented_physical_measurement` — acoustic/photometric/force values / `route_metric` — distances, intervals / `field_observation` / `participatory_spatial` / `stated_unmeasured`), `device_class` CHECK (`manual_self_propelled` / `manual_attendant` / `power_chair` / `scooter` / `bariatric_manual` / `bariatric_power` — the corpus itself distinguishes them (REF-00061; FDR-BAB-04) / `walker_rollator` / `mixed` / `not_device_scoped`), `root_population_note` (the root's sample description — the fit-population test), `file_anchor` (path+section into the md/yaml/json corpus — the tri-format provenance link). Derived view `v_value_independence`: per (parameter, population), COUNT(DISTINCT root_id) excluding `committee_assertion` — **the computable best-practice-vs-consensus discriminator** — under three anti-gaming disciplines (adversarial-review hardening): (a) **root registry**: a `root_id` must reference either a `root_ref_id` in `evidence_sources` or a registered external-root stub (one row per out-of-corpus root, e.g. the 1970s VA/HUD studies), so two extractors cannot mint two ids for one root and double the count; (b) **`untraced` rows carry NULL `root_id`** and are excluded from the count — untraceability can never out-score a traced chain; (c) **`root_classification_basis` + `contested` fields** per row, so the root-vs-echo judgment is recordably disputable, not free-text folklore.
decisions/DR-2026-07-20-evidence-architecture-weighted-strength-reconciliation.md:15:- `tier-system.md §8` (:89–99): *"Every tier can anchor a best-practice claim; the claim's strength is weighted by the tier of its evidence"* — three strength bands via `●◐○`; T4/T5 anchor at `◐` ("current standards practice"), T6/grey at `○` (weak, code-floor).

decisions/DR-2026-07-20-evidence-architecture-weighted-strength-reconciliation.md:30:- `scripts/assess/assess_cell.py:~554` — `CREATE VIEW v_best_practice … WHERE state IN ('stated','provisional') AND code_floor_only = 0 AND tier_basis NOT LIKE '%(regulatory_stratum_only)'`.
decisions/DR-2026-07-20-evidence-architecture-weighted-strength-reconciliation.md:34:**Precedence is not ambiguous.** `evidence-architecture.md:4` carries the clause "where this document and a cited source ever diverge, the cited source governs," and `tier-system.md §8` post-dates the 2026-07-13 ratification. So **in the interim `tier-system.md §8` governs**: every tier may anchor at graded strength *today*. The divergence is therefore not a live behavioral contradiction in doctrine — it is an **unreconciled-text + stale-code** state: the canonical architecture doc and the `v_best_practice` view still describe and enforce the superseded wall.
decisions/DR-2026-07-20-evidence-architecture-weighted-strength-reconciliation.md:36:The one substantive thing precedence does **not** silently fix: `v_best_practice` still *mechanically excludes* regulatory-stratum-only cells. Code behavior and §8 already disagree the moment such a cell would be rendered.
decisions/DR-2026-07-20-evidence-architecture-weighted-strength-reconciliation.md:40:`tier-system.md §8`'s weighted-strength model can be read two ways where it meets `evidence-architecture.md §5/§6`. The owner must pick one, and the choice is genuinely load-bearing because it decides whether a code-only cell can ever carry best-practice framing:

decisions/RATIFICATION-PACKAGE-2026-07-12.md:69:### B4. `DR-2026-05-24-best-practice-supersession-protocol`
governance/adversarial-use-framework.md:11:A guidebook of accessible-design best practices is information. Information can be misused. This document catalogues identifiable misuse patterns, names the mitigations the project's existing methodology already provides, and specifies a pre-release review gate plus a periodic review cadence. It is not a guarantee that misuse can be prevented — that is not within the project's reach. It is a commitment that misuse vectors are surfaced explicitly, that mitigations are designed in deliberately, and that each release is reviewed against the catalogue before publication.
governance/adversarial-use-framework.md:23:**Mechanism.** The guidebook publishes specifications above the regulatory floor (Tier 1–5 evidence-based ranges; best-practice synthesis). An adversarial reader cites the guidebook to argue that a Universal Mode (code-floor) compliant design is *adequate because the guidebook acknowledges Universal Mode exists* — using the guidebook's own structure to justify floor-only design. A variant: citing the conditional language of Tier 1 ("range with median default") to argue that the lower bound of the range is acceptable as a default rather than the median.
governance/adversarial-use-framework.md:29:- Project-standards L20 (amended 2026-07-21, Option A): a best_practice_synthesis derived solely from code consensus is a weak-band (○) "best practice as currently known" claim, valid only flagged code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat — never unflagged or full-strength. The guidebook offers code-only values only as weak-band, flagged best practice, never as full-strength best practice.
governance/adversarial-use-framework.md:31:- Three-tier design hierarchy: Universal Mode is *universal design / code compliance*, never *best practice*. The hierarchy makes the distinction between floor and aspiration unmistakable.
governance/adversarial-use-framework.md:85:**Mitigation.** A6 §1: tier and evidence_type are mechanically validated against the source. EvidenceSource.tier is bounded 1–6; EvidenceSource.evidence_type uses the canonical enum. A6 validators check tier-type consistency. Project-standards L20: a Tier 6 source cannot underpin a best_practice_synthesis.

governance/audience-priority.md:49:Disabled people use environments. Their lived experience tests whether what gets built actually serves them. The guidebook's claim to evidence-based best practice is empty if it is not legible and accountable to the people whose needs it claims to specify.

governance/co1-operational.md:29:Co-1 is the evidence tier that the Evidence Hierarchy designates as **co-primary with Tier 1 OT-intervention clinical research** for purposes of best-practice claims in this guidebook. The full canonical text is in `governance/mission-and-epistemics.md` §Doctrinal commitments §3 and §Epistemic commitments §Evidence hierarchy. This specification operates within that text; it does not revise it.
governance/co1-operational.md:52:A statement by a single disabled person speaking only for themselves is not Co-1 evidence. It is a personal testimony — meaningful, but not Co-1 in the schematic sense. Co-1 requires methodological grounding: peer review, organizational accountability, or scholarly authorship. The distinction matters because Co-1's epistemic weight in best-practice claims rests on this grounding, not on disability identity alone.
governance/co1-operational.md:66:This is not a courtesy; it is a methodological commitment. A best-practice claim in a domain where Co-1 evidence is available, made without engaging Co-1 evidence, is methodologically incomplete. The evidence hierarchy makes this explicit.
governance/co1-operational.md:149:The voice convention for convergence: "Co-1 and Tier 1 evidence converge on [value]: Tier 1 ([source]) shows [Tier 1 finding]; Co-1 ([source/list]) document [Co-1 finding]; the converged best-practice value is [value]."

governance/evidence-architecture.md:44:- **T4–T6** — the **regulatory stratum**: international standards, national beyond-code frameworks, statutory codes. Valid for code-baseline claims and jurisdiction tracking. Under the weighted-strength anchor model (`tier-system.md` §8, superseding §3's binary wall; owner directive 2026-07-21, Option A) **they anchor best-practice claims only at weak weighted strength**: where no anchoring-band evidence (T1/Co-1/T2/Co-2, T3 per its threshold DR) exists, the regulatory value is stated as best practice *as currently known, given that nothing stronger says otherwise* — always flagged weak/code-derived, never unflagged, with the convergence-not-evidence caveat intact: convergence of codes is convergence-not-evidence — the jurisdictions could all be wrong together, or all be copying one unevidenced ancestor.

governance/evidence-architecture.md:78:| T6 statutory code (code) | **Direct** — *is* the floor (DIRECT) | Grain-directness NON-ANCHORING; renders as weak-band best practice only when no anchoring evidence exists — see footnote †† | NON-ANCHORING |
governance/evidence-architecture.md:82:†† **Grain-directness is not render-anchoring (Option A, owner directive 2026-07-21).** Two distinct facts share the word "anchoring" in the code-grain rows, and this matrix keeps them separate. (a) **NON-ANCHORING** is the *mechanical grain-directness outcome* from `schemas/directness.py` (`code×population → NON-ANCHORING`; `code×person → NON-ANCHORING`): code-grain evidence cannot *directly* support a population-specific value. These values are regenerated by the §10 sweep (`scripts/audit/matrix_consistency.py`) and are **unchanged by Option A** — Option A is a render/state-layer decision, not a directness-layer one; any re-grain of `schemas/directness.py` is a separately reviewed follow-up (`decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md` §5). (b) **Render-anchoring at weak weighted strength** is now permitted per `tier-system.md` §8: a determination whose entire basis is the regulatory stratum renders as a weak-band (○), code-derived best-practice determination — always flagged, always carrying the convergence-not-evidence caveat (§5). The Person-Mode cells remain NON-ANCHORING in **both** senses: Person Mode stores no determinations (§5), and no code value resolves an individual's assessed need (`co1-operational.md`). **Band assignment:** any `regulatory_stratum_only` cell renders at ○ weak even where T4/T5 sources are present; §8's ◐ "current standards practice" band applies to individual T4/T5 *citations* within an otherwise-anchored cell, and to source-level ●◐○ markers (`decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md` §2.3).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:90:**G1 — Re-graining rule for T4/T5** `[NEW — DR-gated]`. `tier-system.md` §1 already says T4/T5 sources can support best-practice claims "if the standard itself rests on T1/T2 evidence." This document operationalizes that clause: a T4/T5 source is assigned `GRAIN_AGGREGATE` (and thereby escapes NON-ANCHORING at Population Mode) **only** when its evidence basis is documented as traceable to T1/T2 evidence, with that provenance recorded on the source record. Otherwise it keeps `GRAIN_CODE`. Untraceable standards convergence stays in the regulatory stratum where §2 put it.
governance/evidence-architecture.md:100:Directness conditions how much a source's *claim* weighs. Genealogy conditions how much a value's *apparent agreement* weighs. It gives the project's central adjudication — best practice versus consensus — a computable form. `[H1 — DR-2026-07-13-value-genealogy-and-derivation-handshake, ratified 2026-07-13]`
governance/evidence-architecture.md:116:Each (`parameter_id` × at least one of `identity_code`, `icf_code`, `needs_code`, `medical_code`) cell holds one of four states (`evidence-methodology.md` §2; storage: migration 026): `stated`, `provisional`, `pending`, `not_applicable`. The determination is written by a pure function — same evidence + same `rule_version` ⇒ same state and same `derivation_sha` (`workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §3) — and no `stated` or `provisional` determination can exist without non-empty `governing_refs` (anti-hallucination, DR-2026-07-12 item 10; `pending`/`not_applicable` rows carry a gap link or rationale instead).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:120:- A determination anchored in T1/Co-1/T2/Co-2 (with T3 per its threshold DR) is a **Population-Mode best-practice determination** — the only kind rendered at anchoring strength (`●` band, subject to its conditioning). Since the weighted-strength amendment (owner directive 2026-07-21, Option A; `tier-system.md` §8) the phrase "best practice" is no longer exclusive to it: the weak band below extends the phrase, never the strength (the ◐ band applies at citation level only — footnote ††).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:121:- A determination whose entire basis is the regulatory stratum (T4–6) is a **Universal-Mode regulatory determination**, flagged `regulatory_stratum_only` (extending migration 026's `code_floor_only`, which covers only the T6-only case, to the full T4–6-only case). It answers "what does the harmonised/statutory floor specify?" — and, per owner directive 2026-07-21 (Option A), it **is a best-practice determination at the weak band (○)**: best available *as currently known, given that nothing stronger says otherwise*. It renders in every register, for every audience, **only** flagged weak/code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat — never unflagged, never at anchoring strength (amended I3, §6); its state remains `provisional`, never `stated`. `v_best_practice` includes it as a queryably distinct weak-band row, carrying the row's `marker` (migration 116, which replaced the `strength_band` column of migration 029). This sentence previously read "today the view still excludes `regulatory_stratum_only` rows … and no strength-band column exists", which migration 029 had already made false. The `provisional` confidence flag still names the absent anchoring dimensions (`evidence-methodology.md` §2.3's flag, with teeth at T4/T5, not just T6).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:125:This closes the channel through which the convergence-not-evidence trap could re-enter: previously, `evidence-methodology.md` §2.3 let T4–6 convergence produce a `provisional` synthesis that §3.4's voice line could render as "[N] jurisdictions' standards converge on [value]" without a mandatory scale disclaimer, and the policymaker profile up-weights exactly that stratum. Since Option A (2026-07-21) the closure works by **mandatory flagging, not suppression**: scale-tagging plus the weak/code-derived flag make the laundering path mechanically impossible rather than editorially discouraged — the regulatory value may now be *rendered* as weak-band best practice, but it can never be rendered *unflagged*, never above the register map's `(provisional, regulatory_stratum_only)` row, and never with the convergence-not-evidence caveat dropped — and by amended I3 (§6) that flag-and-caveat requirement binds **every** rendering of the value, not only best-practice-phrased ones.
governance/evidence-architecture.md:165:**Advocacy-brief use-pattern** `[NEW — DR-gated: G5]`. "Advocate" is not currently a named audience anywhere in canonical governance; `armature_v4.md` merges "disabled person / advocate" into one role. Resolution: advocacy is ratified as a **use-pattern within the disabled-person primary audience** (available by extension to carers and DPO staff), not a fifth audience. Its content need is real and distinct: the code-vs-best-practice **delta** ("the law requires 1800mm; the evidence supports 2440mm"), rights framing (CRPD-linked), and citable evidence strength — the policymaker rationale pattern with reversed valence. It inherits primary-audience priority (it belongs to a primary audience), which `audience-priority.md`'s conflict rules already handle; no restructuring of the four-audience architecture is needed, and the delta it foregrounds is computable (`v_code_floor` join per DR-2026-07-12 item 9).

governance/evidence-architecture.md:186:- **I3 (amended 2026-07-21, Option A — absolute form repealed) — Regulatory-stratum-only cells carry no *unflagged* and no *above-weak-band* best-practice language,** in any register, under any weighting profile. **Every rendering of such a cell's value — whether or not it uses best-practice phrasing —** must carry the weak/code-derived flag and the convergence-not-evidence caveat, and no rendering may exceed the register map's `(provisional, regulatory_stratum_only)` row (I5 applies with that row as ceiling; brevity under I5 may shorten wording but may never omit the flag or the caveat).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:204:**8.1 Corridor width (the canonical case, `tier-system.md` §3).** T5/T6 codes converge on 1800mm two-wheelchair passing; Co-1/T2/T3 evidence (Bauman 2010 DeafSpace, Vaughn 2018, Cloete & Rout 2025) anchors 2440mm primary corridors. — *Universal Mode:* floor = 1800mm (code-grain DIRECT); the population evidence justifies specifying above the floor. *Population Mode:* best practice 2440mm at anchoring strength; the 1800mm convergence is grain-directness NON-ANCHORING, and because anchoring-band evidence exists for this cell it is not `regulatory_stratum_only`, so amended I3's weak-band best-practice allowance never applies here: the map row for the anchored tuple contains no floor-as-best-practice language, and I1/I5 forbid exceeding it — so calling 1800mm "best practice" remains forbidden here in every register (weak-band code-derived phrasing exists only for cells with *no* anchoring evidence). *Person Mode:* no re-adjudication of the population value; assessment resolves the individual's own functional need, scaffolded by — not bounded by — the population range (e.g., a wheelchair user with a service animal, or two signing companions, may need the full 2440mm envelope, or more). — *Designer:* "2440mm best practice [●]; 1800mm regulatory floor (AS 1428.1 et al.)." *Policymaker:* the delta, with the convergence-not-evidence warning. *Disabled person:* "Research and Deaf-community experience support corridors wide enough for two people to sign side-by-side or two wheelchairs to pass — wider than the legal minimum." *Advocacy-brief:* "The law requires 1800mm; the evidence supports 2440mm. Citable sources: …" Same tuple in all four (I1).
governance/evidence-architecture.md:206:**8.2 Co-2-only cell (e.g., a home-modification parameter addressed by an RCOT/AOTA CPG, no trial evidence).** State `stated` per §2.2 condition 3; tuple (stated, Co-2, single). Every register discloses the basis honestly (map row 2): the claim is legitimate best practice *and* its evidence character is visible — rigour and usability are not traded off.
governance/evidence-architecture.md:208:**8.3 T4/T5-only cell (the decisive G1 test).** ISO + BS convergence on a parameter, zero T1/Co-1/T2/Co-2. Pre-G1: `provisional`, rendered "standards converge on X," policymaker profile up-weights it — laundering. Post-G1: `provisional` + `regulatory_stratum_only`; every register uses map row 3. Post-Option-A (owner directive 2026-07-21): the cell surfaces in `v_best_practice` as a queryably distinct weak-band (○) code-derived best-practice determination (landed: migration 029's `strength_band`, replaced by the row's `marker` in migration 116) — rendered "best practice as currently known" with the mandatory weak/code-derived flag and convergence-not-evidence caveat; where the ISO and BS floors differ, the direction-aware most-accommodating value anchors (§5); the confidence flag names the absent anchoring dimensions; the cell still appears in the gap register as needing anchoring evidence. If the standard's own evidence base is later documented as traceable to T1/T2, the G1 re-graining rule admits it as GRAIN_AGGREGATE — with provenance recorded, not assumed.
governance/evidence-architecture.md:214:**Rigour.** The architecture's methodological anchors, and where each is argued in full: correspondence to GRADE/JBI/SIGN and why wholesale GRADE adoption is insufficient for built-environment claims (`references/methodology-evidence-hierarchy-mapping.md` v2 — the directness layer *is* GRADE's indirectness domain, applied bidirectionally; synthesis-above-primary follows GRADE; the co-primacy of lived experience is a declared, argued departure, not an oversight); categorical rather than numeric conditioning (no false-precision scores, `schemas/directness.py` BOUNDARY note); determinism and reproducibility (pure determination function, `rule_version` + `derivation_sha`); falsifiability (every `stated`/`provisional` cell records what evidence would overturn it, `workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §3 item 6); absence-of-evidence ≠ evidence-of-absence (Altman & Bland 1995, applied to cells in §2.4 and — via G2 — to the project's own assessment metadata); and the anti-hallucination gate (no determination without resolvable `governing_refs`).
governance/evidence-methodology.md:30:The guidebook draws on tiers T1–T6 of evidence, with two co-primary parallel tracks (Co-1, Co-2). The full hierarchy is defined in `governance/tier-system.md` §1. This section operationalizes it — specifying how each tier is encoded, validated, and used in best-practice synthesis.

governance/evidence-methodology.md:134:The OR clause is deliberate: Co-1 alone is sufficient for `stated`, as is Co-2 alone, as is Tier 2 alone. **Tier 3 alone is not** (tier3-stated-threshold DR): T3-clinical-alone yields `provisional`; T3-grey-alone yields `pending`; Tier 3 contributes to `stated` only through convergence (≥2 evidence axes, one of which may be T3-clinical). This follows from the doctrinal commitments that Co-1 is co-primary with Tier 1 — a best-practice claim grounded solely in strong Co-1 evidence is a legitimate evidence-based claim, not a provisional one — and that Tier 3 is "rarely the sole basis" (`tier-system.md` §1).
governance/evidence-methodology.md:136:**What `stated` does not mean:** `stated` does not mean the claim is beyond dispute. It means the evidence basis is sufficient to make a defensible best-practice claim at the tier cited. A `stated` cell may still be revised when new evidence emerges, when higher-tier evidence replaces lower-tier, or when cross-tier divergence is resolved through new research.
governance/evidence-methodology.md:144:Concretely, `provisional` applies in two cases (per the tier3-stated-threshold DR and unification-DR item G1b, ACCEPTED 2026-07-13): (a) **T3-clinical-alone** — lower-control primary clinical evidence with direct relevance but no anchoring corroboration; and (b) the evidence basis is **Tier 4–6 only** (international standards, national frameworks, or statutory codes) rich enough to produce a qualified synthesis. **Scale-tagging (G1b):** a T4–6-only determination is a *Universal-Mode regulatory determination* — `design_scale='universal'`, flagged `regulatory_stratum_only` (extending `code_floor_only` from the T6-only case). Per owner directive 2026-07-21 (Option A, `decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md`) it is a best-practice determination at the weak band (○): rendered in every register only flagged weak/code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat, never unflagged and never above the weak band (amended invariant I3, `governance/evidence-architecture.md` §6); it is a queryably distinct weak-band row of `v_best_practice`: the DR-2026-07-21 §5 engine follow-up **landed** (commit `ab53f9d5`), the view carries the row's `marker` (migration 116 replaced its `strength_band` column). *This sentence said "until then the view still excludes it" until 2026-08-05 — doctrine understating its own engine, which is the same class of error as doctrine overstating it.* Its state remains `provisional`, never `stated`. governance/legal-regulatory.md:11:The guidebook is a synthesis of accessibility evidence and best practice. It is published into a regulatory environment where building codes, professional standards, and disability-rights statutes operate. This document specifies the project's posture toward that environment: how the guidebook positions itself in liability terms, how it discloses its limits to readers, how it is licensed for use and reuse, where it sits relative to formal jurisdictional adoption, and what questions remain for external counsel review.
governance/legal-regulatory.md:29:A practitioner consulting the guidebook for a corridor-width specification reads: a code-floor value (Tier 6), a best-practice range with convergence assessment (Tier 1–5 synthesis), the populations the value supports, and the divergent positions where they exist. The practitioner then exercises professional judgment to select a value appropriate to the project, the jurisdiction, and the people the building serves. The guidebook supports that judgment; it does not replace it.
governance/legal-regulatory.md:89:> **About this guidebook.** This guidebook is a synthesis of accessibility evidence and best practice produced by an independent author. It is published as a teaching and reference work. It is not a legal authority and does not replace the building codes, accessibility standards, or disability-rights statutes that apply in your jurisdiction. Where the guidebook's recommendations exceed your jurisdiction's code, the code remains the regulatory minimum and the guidebook's value remains a recommendation. Where the guidebook's recommendations and your jurisdiction's code differ, the code is mandatory; the guidebook informs your judgment about how to design above that minimum.

governance/mission-PROVISIONAL.md:47:### 2. Best practice is determined by the evidence hierarchy, not by code consensus
governance/mission-PROVISIONAL.md:49:Tier 1 OT-intervention clinical research, Co-1 lived experience, Co-2 OT CPGs, Tier 3 systematic reviews establish best practice. Tier 6 codes are the compliance floor, not the aspiration. **A best-practice claim derived solely from code consensus is in error.**

governance/mission-and-epistemics.md:42:### 2. Best practice is graded by the evidence hierarchy — code consensus anchors it only at the weak band
governance/mission-and-epistemics.md:44:Best practice is anchored by Tier 1 primary research (OT-prioritized but **not** OT-exclusive — directly-relevant high-control non-OT primary research is admitted at Tier 1, per decision D-E), Co-1 lived experience, Tier 2 synthesis (systematic reviews / meta-analyses and named-organisation evidence-based standards), and Co-2 OT CPGs. Tier 3 lower-control primary clinical evidence is supporting, rarely the sole basis.
governance/mission-and-epistemics.md:46:Tiers 4–6 are a regulatory-baseline stratum: Tier 6 codes are the compliance floor, not the aspiration. **Amended 2026-07-21 (owner directive, Option A; `decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md`):** a best-practice claim derived solely from code consensus — convergence across Tier 4–6 sources — is **not *in error*; it is a weak-band (○) best-practice claim** ("best practice as currently known"), valid only when flagged code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat, never rendered unflagged or above the weak band. (An unflagged or above-weak-band code-consensus best-practice claim *is* still in error.) Convergence is informative (it tells us multiple jurisdictions adopted a value) but is not itself strong evidence: the jurisdictions may be wrong together, or reading from a shared pre-evidence floor that none has updated — which is exactly why the claim sits at the weak band, not why it is excluded.
governance/mission-and-epistemics.md:68:Inclusive design is the positive aspiration. Universal Mode is the compliance baseline. Best practice exceeds Universal Mode wherever evidence supports.
governance/mission-and-epistemics.md:107:**The Design Modes weight the hierarchy asymmetrically by scale.** Universal Mode is a code-floor raised by population-level evidence wherever evidence supports a value above the floor. **The Population scale is where source-ranking does its substantive work** — the evidence hierarchy adjudicates the best-practice value and range there. The Person scale is occupational-therapy resolution of the individual's own functional needs — assessment and clinical-reasoning evidence, not parameter-value-ranking; the population range informs the assessment but does not bound the answer, which may fall outside it (commitment 4). The evidence and design axes therefore remain orthogonal, but under *scale-conditioned weighting*: the axes stay independent, while which evidence carries the most weight is conditioned by the design scale in play. Operational detail is in `governance/evidence-methodology.md` §§1.4, 1.6, 1.7.

governance/tier-system.md:15:| **T1** | Primary research with intervention-level or biomechanical control on the parameter under design | `clinical`, occasionally `grey` for unpublished primary | Best-practice claims that turn on physiological / behavioural / biomechanical mechanism |
governance/tier-system.md:16:| **Co-1** | Disability-led lived-experience publications — DPOs, named-org Deaf / blind / wheelchair-user / mental-health / autism-organisation outputs; CRPD Art. 4.3 consultation outputs | `co1` | Best-practice claims that turn on user-stated preference, dignity, autonomy, or self-determined accommodation. Co-1 ranks alongside T1 because primary-research evidence and lived-experience evidence are non-substitutable on different claim types |
governance/tier-system.md:17:| **T2** | Community-consensus synthesis above primary studies but below international standards. Two streams: (a) systematic reviews / meta-analyses; (b) named-organisation evidence-based standards (DPO guidelines, professional-body standards) | `sr_meta`, `standard_eb`, occasionally `co2` for community-of-professional synthesis | Best-practice claims that turn on synthesised evidence across multiple primary studies, OR on professional-body / DPO position |
governance/tier-system.md:18:| **Co-2** | OT / professional-body CPGs (CAOT, AOTA, RCOT, COTEC, WFOT, national equivalents) | `co2` | Best-practice claims that turn on clinical-professional consensus on rehabilitation or activity-of-daily-living adaptation |
governance/tier-system.md:19:| **T3** | Clinical primary research at lower control level (cross-sectional, observational, qualitative, single-centre) plus grey-literature primary research | `clinical` (predominant), `grey`, occasionally `standard_eb` for specialised technical guidance | Supporting evidence for best-practice claims; rarely the sole basis |
governance/tier-system.md:20:| **T4** | International standards (ISO, IEC, CEN, BSI PAS, EN 81-70, EN 17210, etc.) — published by international bodies; legally significant within their adoption scope | `standard_eb` (predominant) | Code-baseline citations of international harmonisation; supporting evidence for best-practice claims if the standard itself rests on T1/T2 evidence |
governance/tier-system.md:21:| **T5** | National beyond-code frameworks — best-practice guidance issued by national bodies but not legally enforced (BS 8300, DIN 18040 draft, Boverket BBR advisories, national CPGs scoped to BE) | `national_fw` (predominant), `standard_eb` | Code-baseline citations at national best-practice tier; supporting evidence for best-practice claims if the framework rests on T1/T2 evidence |
governance/tier-system.md:22:| **T6** | Statutory code — legally enforceable national / sub-national accessibility code (ADA, AS 1428.1, NZS 4121, EN 81-70 when adopted as legal compliance, NZ Building Code D1/AS1, Approved Document M Vol 2, AS/NZS, GB 50763, MLIT barrier-free law, etc.) | `code` | **Code-baseline citations.** Tier 6 establishes regulatory floor. ⚠ Superseded by §8 + Option A (2026-07-21): T6 anchors a **weak-band (○) "best practice as currently known"** claim, flagged code-derived; the convergence of multiple T6 codes on a value is convergence-not-evidence (see §3), retained as the weak-band honesty rule |
governance/tier-system.md:39:## 3. Best-practice ≠ convergence
governance/tier-system.md:43:**The convergence-not-evidence trap.** A claim of the form "BS 8300, DIN 18040-1, Part M, and AS 1428.1 all specify X" is a **convergence claim across Tier 4–6 sources**. Convergence is informative — it tells us multiple jurisdictions adopted the same value. Convergence is NOT best-practice evidence — the jurisdictions could all be wrong together, or could all be reading from a single shared earlier source that itself rests on no evidence, or could all reflect pre-evidence regulatory floors that no jurisdiction has updated.
governance/tier-system.md:45:**The corridor-width worked example.** Multiple Tier 5/6 codes converge on 1800mm two-wheelchair-passing. Co-1/T2/T3 evidence (DSDG Bauman 2010, DeafScape Vaughn 2018, Cloete & Rout 2025) anchors 2440mm primary corridors. The 1800mm convergence describes the regulatory floor; the 2440mm Co-1/T2/T3 anchor describes best practice. Labelling 1800mm "preferred" or "best practice" because it converges across T5/T6 jurisdictions is the failure mode this section names.
governance/tier-system.md:49:> **⚠ Superseded 2026-07-20 by §8.** The original text here read *"they do not — by themselves — anchor best-practice claims. Best-practice claims require T1, Co-1, T2, or Co-2 evidence."* That binary is replaced by the **weighted-strength anchor model** (§8): a T4–T6-only claim *may* be stated, but is rendered at partial/weak strength with an explicit honesty flag. The convergence-not-evidence principle above is retained as the honesty rule for the weak band.
governance/tier-system.md:86:Enshrined by `decisions/DR-2026-07-20-weighted-strength-anchor-model.md` per owner directive. **Supersedes the binary rule in §3** ("best-practice claims require T1/Co-1/T2/Co-2").
governance/tier-system.md:88:**Every tier can anchor a best-practice claim; the strength of the claim is weighted by the tier of its evidence.** The tiers exist so a reader can grade the confidence behind each proposal — not to gate whether a proposal may be stated. Three strength bands (the `●◐○` markers of §5, now with anchoring semantics):
governance/tier-system.md:100:Convergence-not-evidence (§3) is retained as the honesty rule within the weak band. **Amended 2026-07-21 (owner directive, Option A; `decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md`):** a T4–T6-only determination *is* rendered as **weak-band (○) "best practice as currently known"** — best available given that nothing stronger says otherwise — always flagged weak/code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat, never unflagged and never above the ○ band. This supersedes the prior wording ("never relabelled 'best practice'"): such a claim is now stated-and-flagged as weak-band best practice, not suppressed and not merely labelled "regulatory floor," when no stronger evidence exists. **Band assignment at determination level:** a cell whose entire basis is T4–T6 takes ○ even where T4/T5 sources are present; the ◐ "current standards practice" band in the table above applies to individual T4/T5 *citations* within an otherwise-anchored cell, and to source-level `●◐○` markers (§5), which are unchanged (DR-2026-07-21 §2.3).
governance/time-model.md:69:Without a temporal model, the rule history is opaque to mechanical analysis. A skill cannot ask "what was the rule on best-practice synthesis at 2026-03-25" or "which rules currently in effect are older than 90 days and should be reviewed". A9 adds the model; the converter (§7) extracts every RULE block and emits a YAML record per rule.

references/audits/bpc-audit-pass0-2026-05-10.md:16:**Sharpened this session — Convergence ≠ Evidence:** Most-cited / highest-convergence indicates how many sources agree. It does not establish what is best for the disabled person. A converged value qualifies as best practice only if at least one of the following holds:
references/audits/bpc-audit-pass0-2026-05-10.md:24:If the only basis is "N standards agree on this number," the value is a **code-consensus default**, not best practice. It must be relabelled as such.
references/audits/bpc-audit-pass0-2026-05-10.md:31:3. At each increment, accept the value as a candidate best-practice if a **Tier 3 or better** source supports it for the population. Tier 3 or better = Tier 1 (OT clinical research), Co-1 (lived experience), Tier 2 (NGO/DPO advocacy), Co-2 (OT CPG), or Tier 3 (systematic reviews / meta-analyses + OT body guidelines). Tier 4 (international standards), Tier 5 (national beyond-code), and Tier 6 (codes) **never raise the ceiling**.

references/audits/bpc-audit-pass0-2026-05-10.md:253:**Independent finding worth investigating:** The IDeA Center figure cited in the same BPC says "Power wheelchair users require 1400×1600mm minimum for independent use." 1400mm WIDTH is not present in any of the BPC's "best practice" values (which keep car width at 1100mm). references/audits/methodological-audit_2026-04-30.md:38:**Evidence stratification.** Seven-tier hierarchy with Co-1 (lived experience) elevated to co-primary alongside Tier 1 OT clinical RCTs; codes demoted to Tier 6 floor. The explicit rejection of code-consensus as best practice is the most consequential single methodological move in the doctrine. The treatment of code minima as "informative where nothing else can be" rather than as gold standards is uncommon in the field.

references/audits/process-audit_2026-04-30.md:47:Block 33 (2026-04-24/25) declared that the project's then-current form (markdown specifications + Appendix A tables + BPC `best_practice_synthesis` derived from code consensus) is the wrong long-term medium. The synthesis workplan reframes ~280+ prior commits as input data, not deliverables to patch. Stage C migration is budgeted at 140–200 sessions / 12–18 months.
references/audits/process-audit_2026-04-30.md:49:**Why this matters for long-term health.** A pivot of this scale is the right call once the corpus contamination diagnosis was accepted, but it creates three durable risks: (a) the kept-research / retired-form boundary lives only in the synthesis workplan and a few session notes — not in a single canonical "what survives migration" register; (b) future contributors (or future Claude sessions) may not realise that a guidance value extracted from a retired BPC `best_practice_synthesis` is now suspect; (c) the migration estimate is an Opus session's best guess at a moment of high context — there is no second-source check on it.

references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:164:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:169:Best-practice provision: RT60 ≤ 0.3 s in classrooms ≤ 283 m³. This requirement is now peer-reviewed (Iglehart, 2020, AJA) and formally encoded in ANSI/ASA S12.60-2010/Part 1 Footnote e. It applies to children with hearing aids or cochlear implants. The standard general-population requirement (0.6 s) is demonstrably insufficient for hearing device users; speech perception scores are significantly better at 0.3 s than 0.6 s for this population. Background noise target: ≤ 30–35 dB(A) (more stringent than the standard ≤ 35 dB(A)).
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:172:Best-practice provision: No internationally agreed quantified RT60 target specific to autistic users. Bettarello et al. (2021, *Applied Sciences*) and Caniato et al. (2024) confirm that existing standards (ANSI/ASA S12.60, BB93, UNI 11532-2) are calibrated to neurotypical populations and are insufficient for NDV/AUT. Autistic users are significantly more affected by modest background noise increases (52→55 dB(A)) and by sudden noise events than neurotypical users. Evidence supports: lower background noise targets (≤ 35 dB(A); aspiration: ≤ 30 dB(A)), broadband absorption to suppress flutter echo and reflective peaks, no sound masking systems (A-13 doctrine), and sensory gradient zoning (F-01). Sound masking is contraindicated for NDV/AUT.
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:175:Best-practice provision: Acoustic calm as primary therapeutic intervention. BrainXchange (Canada, Tier 2 Co-1) documents background noise management as clinically significant for dementia care. Devos et al. (2019, PMC) confirm RT60 measurement and acoustic intervention as standard practice in Belgian nursing homes, with measurable outcomes. Lyngby-Taarbæk case study (Part IX-06) documents 40% reduction in agitation events following acoustic ceiling installation. Targets consistent with NDV: NC-25 (≤ 35 dB(A)), RT60 ≤ 0.5 s in occupied common areas.
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:178:Best-practice provision: Flicker-free, low background noise, NC-25 or better. No specific RT60 target for PCS confirmed beyond general accessibility guidance. The PCS population is absent from all room acoustic standards reviewed in any language.
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:259:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ENV.md:275:### Best-practice synthesis

references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:3:# Best Practices Compendium — ALL-ROOMS
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:31:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:86:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:101:| Zero-threshold shower mandatory or best practice | EN, DE, NO, SV, FR, NL, IT, PT, JA | 22/24 | 5–6 |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:152:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:213:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:218:**Conflict resolution:** Ramp-only access serves MOB/WC well but disadvantages MOB/AMB (ambulant disabled), OFS, and NEU populations who often prefer steps with handrail. Best practice provides both ramp and complementary stepped route where site permits. This is the Scottish Technical Handbook requirement and RCOT best practice recommendation.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:220:**Highest-ambition actionable specification:** Level approach (gradient ≤1:50 entire path, cross-fall ≤1:48); zero threshold at entrance door; minimum clear door width ≥900mm (best practice) / ≥850mm (minimum); level landing ≥1500×1500mm outside door clear of arc; automatic door opener provided; tactile/visual attention indicator at entrance; luminance contrast ≥0.4 (entrance vs surroundings). Where level access cannot be achieved: permanent integrated ramp ≤1:20, ≥900mm clear width, bilateral handrails, rest landing every 750mm rise or 10m run (whichever comes first), luminance contrast marking at ramp top and bottom.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:231:| Automatic door openers are best practice at main entrances | NO (§12-4), UK, CA, AU | EN, NO | 5 |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:292:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:295:**Most targeted provision:** Thermally broken accessible threshold unit (proprietary sealed threshold with weather bar embedded below floor surface), achieving 0mm internal-to-external level difference with drainage channel, tested to thermal performance standards. This represents the best-practice resolution of the tension between weather protection and accessibility.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:342:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:347:**Conflict resolution:** The ADA side-reach maximum was 1370mm (1991 ADAAG); LPA advocacy and ANSI A117.1 lowered it to 1220mm. This is a Co-1-driven improvement. Best practice adopts 1220mm as the absolute maximum regardless of reach type. Lower (1100mm) is best practice for the most constrained users. The NBR 9050 lateral reach-with-trunk-displacement envelope extends to higher values but this represents functional compensation, not design intent — design should eliminate the need for trunk displacement.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:349:**Door hardware:** Lever handle or U-shaped pull mandatory. Round door knobs prohibited (require wrist twist — functionally inaccessible for significant proportion of UPL and arthritis population). Automatic door openers best practice for all entrance doors.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:364:| Switches/sockets 450–1200mm; best practice 450–1050mm | UK (BS 8300) | EN | 5 |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:375:| Door force max | CA: ≤38N | ADA: no max specified (fire doors excepted) | Regulatory gap in US; CA best practice fills it |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:430:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:463:| Outdoor loop routes | AU/NZ (secure garden loop = normative best practice) | ZH/KO (outdoor access less common; safety primary) | Cultural care model differences |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:527:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:531:- Best practice: 1100mm × 1800mm (stretcher-capable, large power wheelchair); BS 8300 recommends 1100mm × 2000mm for new buildings.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:539:- Two wheelchair users passing: ≥1525mm (ADA); ≥1800mm (UK Part M non-residential best practice; care settings standard).
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:540:- Best practice for primary routes: 1800mm minimum, with 1800×1800mm passing places at maximum 10m intervals where narrowing occurs.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:546:- Best practice: rest seating at ≤25–30m intervals on primary circulation routes; reclining or tilt option for OFS populations (no standard; expert consensus only — THIN-BASE).
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:550:- Sliding automatic doors eliminate operating force barrier entirely — best practice for primary entrances (BS 8300; DIN 18040-1; ADA; TEK17; DIN/EN all recommend).
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:552:- Operating force where manual: ≤20N (AU AS 1428.1; NO TEK17 — best practice); ≤30N preferred (OT Tier 1 evidence per MOB BPC Steinfeld et al. 2016); ≤38N maximum (CMHC; ADA guidance).
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:554:**Most inclusive provision:** Automatic sliding doors on all primary routes; EN 81-70 Type 2 minimum lift (1100×1400mm) with best practice 1100×1800mm; 1800mm corridor width on primary routes; rest seating at 25–30m intervals with tilt/recline option.
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:564:| Automatic sliding doors on primary accessible routes = best practice | EN, DE, SV, NO, FR | UK, DE, SE, NO, FR, AU, US | Tier 6/5 (all surveyed standards recommend; BS 8300 §7; DIN 18040) |
references/bpc/ALL-ROOMS.md:571:| Corridor minimum non-residential | UK Part M (1200mm min; 1800mm two-way) | DE DIN 18040-1 (1200mm min; 1800mm recommended) | Aligned on best practice; differ at minimum |

references/bpc/DBL.md:3:# Best Practices Compendium — DBL
references/bpc/DBL.md:39:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/DBL.md:41:**Most inclusive provision:** Tactile floor wayfinding (TWSI/ISO 23599:2019; ABNT NBR 16537:2024) throughout all primary circulation routes, with continuity guaranteed for DBL users who rely on cane and tactile cues simultaneously. Tactile building orientation maps at all primary building entrances at 900–1200mm AFF. Intervenor/guide-interpreter clear floor zone of ≥1500mm adjacency at all service counters and communication nodes. Vibrotactile alert systems (vibrating pagers, floor transducers) throughout all occupied spaces as complement to visual strobes — no built-environment standard in any jurisdiction mandates this, but DbI best practice guidelines and DeafBlind information sources confirm the design requirement.
references/bpc/DBL.md:58:| Vibrotactile alerting (pager/bed shaker) confirmed as best practice for DBL sleeping accommodation | EN (DbI guidelines, DeafBlind-Info AU) | Tier 2 |

references/bpc/DEAF.md:3:# Best Practices Compendium — DEAF
references/bpc/DEAF.md:30:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/DEAF.md:115:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/DEAF.md:121:**Conflict resolution:** IEC 60118-4 requires hearing loop provision; Auracast standard expected 2027 will complement, not replace, loops during transition. Best practice is dual provision: loop + Auracast-ready infrastructure. Loop decommissioning before Auracast is fully adopted would eliminate access for the majority of hearing aid users who lack Auracast-compatible devices.
references/bpc/DEAF.md:123:**Cross-language convergence:** RT60 ≤ 0.4 s is the consensus best-practice target across all 14 languages and their governing standards (DIN 18041, BS 8300, BBR, NS 8175, BR18, Asetus 241, CTE HR, NBR 10152, NEN 9120 equivalent, Korean school standard). IEC 60118-4 is universally adopted as the hearing loop performance standard across all jurisdictions — the highest cross-language convergence of any DEAF-related specification.
references/bpc/DEAF.md:132:| RT60 ≤ 0.4 s in speech communication spaces (best practice) | EN, SV, NO, DA, FI, FR, DE, NL, ES, PT, KO, IT | Tier 4–5 |
references/bpc/DEAF.md:133:| STI ≥ 0.50 minimum; ≥ 0.60 best practice | EN, FR, DE (DIN 18041) | Tier 4 |
references/bpc/DEAF.md:180:**Best-practice provision:**
references/bpc/DEAF.md:182:- Room loop (A-11): all assembly spaces ≥25 capacity (UK/ISO baseline); all ERP cat. 1–4 and spaces ≥50 seats (FR CEREMA — stricter best practice)
references/bpc/DEAF.md:186:- Trigger threshold: guidebook ≥25 capacity vs FR CEREMA ≥50 seats + all ERP cat. 1–4. CEREMA is more comprehensive; flag as cross-jurisdictional best practice in A-11.

references/bpc/MOB.md:10:**Updated:** 2026-03-19 23:45  **Evidence tier range:** Co-1 – Tier 6  **Status:** COMPLETE — all 24 jurisdictions Co-1 attempted (BE Tier 5 only outstanding); all 14 languages SEARCHED (ES THIN); 1524mm REJECTED as best-practice floor; floor = 1700mm
references/bpc/MOB.md:19:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/MOB.md:22:**Most targeted provision:** Differentiated specification across device type — manual wheelchair (1500 mm minimum / 1700 mm best practice), power wheelchair (1700–1800 mm), scooter and large power device (1800+ mm) — with OT-assessed co-design resolving the specific value at Tier 2. Habinteg Inclusive Housing Design Guide (2024, RCOTSS-Housing OT authored) is the highest-authority source for room-by-room residential turning space targets.
references/bpc/MOB.md:26:**Highest-ambition actionable specification:** Turning space ≥1800 mm diameter [Co-1/Tier 1 — IDeA Center / VA / Habinteg/RCOTSS]; 1700 mm acceptable where space-constrained [Tier 5 — RHFAC CA]; 1700 mm minimum floor [Tier 5 — RHFAC CA / BS8300 UK]. Note: ADA 1524 mm rejected as a design baseline — cited here as evidence of regulatory lag only, not as a permitted specification value. Ramp gradient: 1:20 (5%) ideal [Tier 5]; 1:16 intermediate; 1:12 (8.3%) code minimum with rest platforms every 9 m [Tier 6]. Door clear opening: 900 mm best practice; 850 mm minimum for power WC (CSA B651). SIA 500:2009 (CH): ramp max 6%; admissible to 12% with handrails. NCC 2022/BCA 2024 (AU): AS 1428.1:2021 turning circle 1500 mm × 1500 mm (Tier 6); Summer Foundation/UTS research confirms AS 1428.1 under-accommodates power WC users — same pattern as US. BCA Code 2025 (SG, effective Nov 2025): now includes anthropometric data for power WC users; wheelchair manoeuvring space requirements updated. DL 163/2006 (PT): 1500 mm turning circle for manual WC only — explicitly acknowledged as insufficient for electric WC by authors. DM 236/89 (IT): accessibility/visitability/adaptability three-tier framework; turning space 1500 mm minimum, no power WC provision. 편의증진법 2024 (KR): major 2024 revision shifts from route access to daily life access; 1500 mm manoeuvre space for WC; noted as manual WC basis. NBR 9050:2020 (BR): 360° rotation = 1500 mm diameter; 180° = 1500 mm × 1200 mm; explicitly uses M.R. (módulo de referência) 800 × 1200 mm. SBi-anvisning 230/BR18 (DK): three-tier A/B/C quality levels; C = code minimum (BR18); B = comfort/best practice for WC users; A = care/residential level. ESKEH (FI): Invalidiliitto accessibility audit framework; pyörätuoli kääntöpaikka 1500 mm minimum; sähköpyörätuoli 2500 mm outdoor turning recommended. CEUD Building for Everyone (IE): NDA/CEUD series; Building Regulations Part M 2022 (latest includes Changing Places). NZS 4121:2001 (NZ): 1500 mm diameter manoeuvring circle; G3/AS1 acceptable solution.
references/bpc/MOB.md:36:| Door clear opening: 900 mm best practice; 850 mm minimum | EN, DE, NL | CA/CSA, UK/BS8300, DE/DIN18040 | Tier 5–6 |
references/bpc/MOB.md:55:| Turning circle upgrade: conservative (1500mm) vs. best practice (1800mm) | Most statutory codes (1500mm) | VA/PVA/RHFAC/IDeA (1700–1830mm) | Regulatory lag — 1970s anthropometric data not yet superseded in most codes |
references/bpc/MOB.md:116:**Key finding — SG addendum:** BCA Code 2025 (eff. Nov 2025) incorporates anthropometric data for power WC users and other persons with disabilities — explicit recognition that prior code insufficient for contemporary device footprints. Aligns with IDeA Center 1700-1800mm best-practice tier.

references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/assistive-listening-systems.md:13:**Best-practice provision:**
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/assistive-listening-systems.md:15:- Room loop (A-11): all assembly spaces ≥25 capacity (UK/ISO baseline); all ERP cat. 1–4 and spaces ≥50 seats (FR CEREMA — stricter best practice)
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/assistive-listening-systems.md:18:**Opus 4.6 synthesis note (2026-03-29):** Synthesis reviewed and confirmed. Two findings. (1) The Japan gap (GAP-ALS-01) is the most significant jurisdiction finding: Japan is the only major jurisdiction with no mandatory hearing loop provision. This should be explicitly noted in A-11 as a jurisdiction-specific gap, not silently omitted. The guidebook's specification should state the IEC 60118-4 requirement as universal best practice while noting Japan's voluntary-only status. (2) The Auracast transition is correctly handled as dual-provision DAR. The critical design decision is: Auracast infrastructure (conduit + power) must be specified at DD stage even though IEC 60118-17 is not published. The cost of retrofit conduit after construction is 10–40× the cost of DD-stage provision. This is a DAR principle application — cross-reference Part 10 §10.1. The CEREMA trigger threshold (≥50 seats + all ERP cat. 1–4) is more comprehensive than the guidebook's ≥25 capacity. Recommend adopting the CEREMA threshold as the guidebook's best-practice standard. Evidence confidence: HIGH for loop provisions (IEC 60118-4, Tier 4, universally adopted); MODERATE for Auracast provisions (no IEC standard until 2027; product-stage evidence only).
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/assistive-listening-systems.md:21:- Trigger threshold: guidebook ≥25 capacity vs FR CEREMA ≥50 seats + all ERP cat. 1–4. CEREMA is more comprehensive; flag as cross-jurisdictional best practice in A-11.
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/deaf-acoustic-built-environment.md:8:**KNOWN CONFLICT (Q24, `workplan/ratification-execution-register-2026-07-13.md`):** this file's "STI ≥0.50 minimum; ≥0.60 best practice" framing conflicts with `room-acoustic-performance.md`'s "STI ≥0.60 general / ≥0.75 DEAF-CI" and `acoustics-speech-intelligibility-disability.md`'s flat "STI ≥0.5". Do not resolve by inspection or point-fix ahead of reverification — all three are in the same PRE-REHABILITATION cohort above; flagged for Phase E.2g to adjudicate together.
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/deaf-acoustic-built-environment.md:28:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/communication-and-alerts/deaf-acoustic-built-environment.md:30:**Most inclusive provision:** Hearing loop systems compliant with IEC 60118-4:2014+AMD1:2017 (field strength ±3 dB across listening plane, SNR ≥47 dB(A) above magnetic noise floor) to be installed in all assembly, reception, service counter, and primary communication spaces. RT60 in speech communication spaces not to exceed 0.4 s at 500 Hz–4 kHz (DIN 18041:2016 Nutzungsart A3/A4; BS 8300-2:2018 §11). Background noise criterion NC-25 or below in hearing-critical spaces. STI ≥ 0.60 in all looped spaces. Auracast (Bluetooth LE Audio) infrastructure provision to be included in all new assembly spaces as a DAR provision for technology transition. [IEC 60118-17 expected late 2027 — confirmed March 2026; dual provision best practice during transition.]

references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:28:### Best-practice synthesis

references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:39:- **Code-floor values (Tier 6 — not best practice):** 915mm continuous single passage (ADA); 900mm residential single passage (DIN 18040-1, BS 8300); 1200mm non-residential single passage (UK Part M, DIN 18040-1); 1525mm two-WC passing (ADA). Adequate where DEAF population is not anticipated and only wheelchair-passing geometry governs.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:41:- **Tier Co-1 / Tier 2 / Tier 3 best practice (DEAF-inclusive primary corridors):** 2440mm primary corridors with glazed/transparent intersections (DSDG Bauman 2010 Co-1; DeafScape Vaughn 2018 Tier 2; Cloete & Rout 2025 Acta Structilia 32(2):238–263 Tier 3 cross-cultural scoping review). Required where signed conversation is anticipated; serves wheelchair-passing simultaneously. 1830mm (6 ft) acceptable on secondary corridors only. 3050mm (10 ft) public exterior pathways where signing groups anticipated.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:48:- Best practice: rest seating at ≤25–30m intervals on primary circulation routes; reclining or tilt option for OFS populations (no standard; expert consensus only — THIN-BASE).
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:52:- Sliding automatic doors eliminate operating force barrier entirely — best practice for primary entrances (BS 8300; DIN 18040-1; ADA; TEK17; DIN/EN all recommend).
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:54:- Operating force where manual: ≤20N (AU AS 1428.1; NO TEK17 — best practice); ≤30N preferred (OT Tier 1 evidence per MOB BPC Steinfeld et al. 2016); ≤38N maximum (CMHC; ADA guidance).
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:56:**Most inclusive provision:** Automatic sliding doors on all primary routes; EN 81-70 Type 2 minimum lift (1100×1400mm) with best practice 1100×1800mm; **2440mm corridor width on primary mixed-population routes with glazed intersections** (converging DEAF Co-1 and Steinfeld 2006 Tier 3 evidence); 1830mm secondary corridors; rest seating at 25–30m intervals with tilt/recline option.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:66:| Automatic sliding doors on primary accessible routes = best practice | EN, DE, SV, NO, FR | UK, DE, SE, NO, FR, AU, US | Tier 6/5 (all surveyed standards recommend; BS 8300 §7; DIN 18040) |
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/accessible-circulation-geometry.md:73:| Corridor minimum non-residential | UK Part M (1200mm min; 1800mm two-way) | DE DIN 18040-1 (1200mm min; 1800mm recommended) | Aligned on **code-consensus value of 1800mm two-way**; 1200mm single-passage code floor. Code consensus is not best practice per project-standards line 20. Best practice = 2440mm primary mixed-population per DEAF Co-1 evidence; 1800mm reflects pre-DEAF-evidence two-WC-passing convergence. |
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/floor-vibration-wheelchair-disability.md:28:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/residential-entry-and-threshold.md:18:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/residential-entry-and-threshold.md:23:**Conflict resolution:** Ramp-only access serves MOB/WC well but disadvantages MOB/AMB (ambulant disabled), OFS, and NEU populations who often prefer steps with handrail. Best practice provides both ramp and complementary stepped route where site permits. This is the Scottish Technical Handbook requirement and RCOT best practice recommendation.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/residential-entry-and-threshold.md:25:**Highest-ambition actionable specification:** Level approach (gradient ≤1:50 entire path, cross-fall ≤1:48); zero threshold at entrance door; minimum clear door width ≥900mm (best practice) / ≥850mm (minimum); level landing ≥1500×1500mm outside door clear of arc (see code-floor disclosure below); automatic door opener provided; tactile/visual attention indicator at entrance; luminance contrast ≥0.4 (entrance vs surroundings). Where level access cannot be achieved: permanent integrated ramp ≤1:20, ≥900mm clear width, bilateral handrails, rest landing every 750mm rise or 10m run (whichever comes first), luminance contrast marking at ramp top and bottom.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/residential-entry-and-threshold.md:27:**Code-floor disclosure:** The 850mm door minimum is the CSA B651-23 code value for power-WC clear width (Tier 6). 900mm is the BS 8300 / DIN 18040-1 standards value (Tier 5). Power-WC + assistant configurations (assistant beside, not behind) require ≥1000mm clear per Habinteg Wheelchair Housing Design Guide 2018 (Tier Co-2 — RCOT/Habinteg authored). Where assistant-accompanied entry is anticipated, 900mm is sub-best-practice; specify ≥1000mm.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/residential-entry-and-threshold.md:42:| Automatic door openers are best practice at main entrances | NO (§12-4), UK, CA, AU | EN, NO | 5 |
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/stair-ramp-threshold-biomechanics-accessibility.md:43:  - Note: 30% LRV standard lacks a published evidence basis for current threshold (Manandhar et al. 2022) — highest achievable contrast recommended as interim best practice
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/stair-ramp-threshold-biomechanics-accessibility.md:65:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-and-level-access.md:16:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-and-level-access.md:19:**Most targeted provision:** Thermally broken accessible threshold unit (proprietary sealed threshold with weather bar embedded below floor surface), achieving 0mm internal-to-external level difference with drainage channel, tested to thermal performance standards. This represents the best-practice resolution of the tension between weather protection and accessibility.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:1:# Best Practices Compendium Entry
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:23:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:26:**Most targeted provision:** DE Nullschwelle doctrine (0 mm, not 20 mm) represents the highest-ambition standard globally — confirmed by 24-jurisdiction review. AU AS 1428.1:2021 adds D-handle requirement for sliding doors and specifies lever return ≥20 mm from door face. UK BS 8300:2018 provides the most detailed ironmongery specification (lever diameter, grip zone, backset, visual contrast). US ADA 2010 is most prescriptive on operating force (≤5 lbf universal). FR (≤50 N) is a significant outlier — more than double the best-practice value.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:28:**Conflict resolution:** Threshold: DE/NO/JP (0 mm) > BR/AU (≤5 mm) > US/CA/SG/ISO (≤13 mm) > UK/SE/CN (≤15 mm) > FR/NL/DK/FI/ES/PT/KR/EU (≤20 mm) > NO-T6/DK-T6/IT/CH (≤25 mm). Handle height: DE (850–1050 mm) and AU (900–1100 mm) are tightest; FR/IT (upper 1300 mm) and US (865–1220 mm) are widest. Operating force: AU/NO (≤20 N) strictest; FR (≤50 N) loosest. Best practice: DE on threshold (0 mm), DE lower + AU upper on handle height (850–1100 mm), AU/NO on force (≤20 N), US on closer sweep (≥5 s).
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:33:(1) Zero-threshold hierarchy (DE/NO/JP 0 mm → BR/AU ≤5 mm → US/CA ≤13 mm → UK/SE ≤15 mm → FR/EU ≤20 mm) is well-documented and the DE Nullschwelle doctrine is correctly identified as global best practice.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:34:(2) Force range: AU/NO ≤20 N as best practice is sound. FR ≤50 N is a genuine outlier (2.5× best practice) and should be noted in any specification as below international best practice.
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:59:| Operating force | AU: ≤20 N · NO: ≤20 N (strictest) | FR: ≤50 N (outlier — 2.5× best practice) · UK Part M: ≤30 N · KR/IT: ≤30 N | Regulatory — FR significantly out of step with international best practice |
references/bpc/entrances-and-circulation/threshold-door-hardware.md:78:Finding: 30 N door opening force acceptable to 94.7% of wheelchair users; 40 N acceptable to 92.1%. Walker users: 30 N = 100%; 40 N = 87.5%. Implication: AU/NO ≤20 N specification is conservative relative to empirical 30 N acceptability threshold — this is appropriate (best practice exceeds minimum acceptable). FR ≤50 N falls above the empirical acceptability curve for a substantial minority of users — confirms the BPC classification of FR as a significant outlier. The Opus synthesis note on FR ≤50 N is empirically supported by this source.

references/bpc/frameworks-and-methodology/cross-population-case-studies.md:31:## best_practice_synthesis
references/bpc/frameworks-and-methodology/cross-population-conflict-resolutions.md:27:  10. **Corridor width: DEAF vs NDV/MH vs DEM (BPC audit Pass 2C, 2026-05-10).** Apparent conflict dissolves under Step-0 variable-conflation check (RULE 2026-03-30 23:30): DEAF requires corridor *cross-section width* ≥2440mm + glazed intersections (DSDG Bauman 2010 Co-1; Vaughn 2018 Tier 2; Cloete & Rout 2025 Tier 3); NDV/MH requires *visual sightline geometry* (no ambush points — satisfied by glazed intersections); DEM requires *building plan topology* (loop plan — operates at building-plan level, independent of corridor cross-section; Marquardt & Schmieg 2009 Tier 3). Resolution: primary mixed-population corridors ≥2440mm clear width with glazed (visually-permeable) intersections at all corridor junctions. 2440mm simultaneously satisfies wheelchair-passing, two-signer conversation, and Steinfeld 2006 entire-sample 180° turning evidence. Bounded compartmentation (residential clusters, retreat zones, sensory rooms) accessed FROM primary circulation, not within it. Synthesis approach: convergent — variables disambiguated. Residual: DEM "loop plan" literature sometimes implies narrow corridor; clinical evidence (Marquardt & Schmieg 2009) addresses topology, not narrowness. Cost: 2440mm uses more floor area than 1800mm — Population-Mode trade-off, not a best-practice argument. Residential applications: domestic single-occupancy → Person Mode occupant-co-designed.
