references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:60:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:62:**Most inclusive provision:** Tactile floor wayfinding (TWSI/ISO 23599:2019; ABNT NBR 16537:2024) throughout all primary circulation routes, with continuity guaranteed for DBL users who rely on cane and tactile cues simultaneously. Tactile building orientation maps at all primary building entrances at 900–1200mm AFF. Intervenor/guide-interpreter clear floor zone of ≥1500mm adjacency at all service counters and communication nodes. Vibrotactile alert systems (vibrating pagers, floor transducers) throughout all occupied spaces as complement to visual strobes — no built-environment standard in any jurisdiction mandates this, but DbI best practice guidelines and DeafBlind information sources confirm the design requirement.
references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:73:**Opus 4.6 synthesis note (2026-03-29):** Synthesis reviewed across all three merged sections. Four findings. (1) THIN-BASE confirmation: zero Tier 1 evidence for any DBL built-environment specification in any language. GAP-DBL-BE-01 (P1 OPEN) correctly flags this. All DBL specifications must carry THIN-BASE disclosure. This is not remediable by further literature search — the evidence does not exist. The population is too small and heterogeneous for RCT-level research to be feasible. Lived experience evidence (Co-1) is co-primary here per project-standards. (2) The Protactile spatial requirement (≤600mm face-to-face, armless chairs, ≥1500mm per communication pair) is a genuinely novel provision not found in any building standard worldwide. It derives from a single community source (Clark/Nuccio). Despite the thin evidence base, the specification is biomechanically grounded (tactile communication requires physical contact; physical contact requires proximity; proximity requires clear floor space). Recommend: specify as Co-1 with THIN-BASE and frame as “emerging best practice” rather than “established provision.” (3) The JA close-range signing (接近手話, 500–1000mm) spatial requirement is distinct from ASL/BSL spatial requirements and from Protactile. This confirms that DBL spatial design cannot be calibrated from a single signed language’s proxemics — consistent with the deaf-spatial-design Opus synthesis finding. (4) CONFLICT: vibrotactile floor systems (for DBL alerting) vs TWSI tactile paving (for VIS/DBL wayfinding) is correctly identified. The synthesis correctly resolves this (TWSI takes precedence; vibrotactile via wearable pager). No further conflict domains identified. Evidence confidence: LOW (all specifications Tier 2–5; zero Tier 1).
references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:81:| Vibrotactile alerting (pager/bed shaker) confirmed as best practice for DBL sleeping accommodation | EN (DbI guidelines, DeafBlind-Info AU) | Tier 2 |
references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:105:| DbI best practice guidelines | Forward | ACVREP CDBIS scope; Nordic ledsagarservice documentation |
references/bpc/population-general/deafblind-built-environment-design.md:111:| DBL-01 | Deafblind International (DbI) | n.d. | Guidelines on Best Practice for Service Provision to Deafblind People | 2 | INT | https://www.deafblindinternational.org/?media_dl=3219 |

references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:15:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:18:**Most targeted provision:** Differentiated specification across device type — manual wheelchair (1500 mm minimum / 1700 mm best practice), power wheelchair (1700–1800 mm), scooter and large power device (1800+ mm) — with OT-assessed co-design resolving the specific value at Tier 2. Habinteg Inclusive Housing Design Guide (2024, RCOTSS-Housing OT authored) is the highest-authority source for room-by-room residential turning space targets.
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:22:**Highest-ambition actionable specification:** Turning space ≥1800 mm diameter for typical power-WC accommodation [Co-1/Tier 2 — IWA BPAG p.29; Tier 5 — BS 8300-2]; **2400 mm where IDEA + BS8300 entire-sample evidence applies (Steinfeld 2006, Tier 3)**, particularly for settings serving bariatric or large-power-WC users; 1925 mm acceptable where UDI sub-sample 180° methodology applies (open-ended bay; Steinfeld 2006); 1700 mm acceptable where space-constrained [Tier 5 — RHFAC CA]; 1700 mm minimum floor [Tier 5 — RHFAC CA / BS8300 UK]. Note: ADA 1524 mm rejected as a design baseline — cited here as evidence of regulatory lag only, not as a permitted specification value. Ramp gradient: 1:20 (5%) ideal [Tier 5]; 1:16 intermediate; 1:12 (8.3%) code minimum with rest platforms every 9 m [Tier 6]. Door clear opening: 900 mm best practice; 850 mm minimum for power WC (CSA B651). SIA 500:2009 (CH): ramp max 6%; admissible to 12% with handrails. NCC 2022/BCA 2024 (AU): AS 1428.1:2021 turning circle 1500 mm × 1500 mm (Tier 6); Summer Foundation/UTS research confirms AS 1428.1 under-accommodates power WC users — same pattern as US. BCA Code 2025 (SG, effective Nov 2025): now includes anthropometric data for power WC users; wheelchair manoeuvring space requirements updated. DL 163/2006 (PT): 1500 mm turning circle for manual WC only — explicitly acknowledged as insufficient for electric WC by authors. DM 236/89 (IT): accessibility/visitability/adaptability three-tier framework; turning space 1500 mm minimum, no power WC provision. 편의증진법 2024 (KR): major 2024 revision shifts from route access to daily life access; 1500 mm manoeuvre space for WC; noted as manual WC basis. NBR 9050:2020 (BR): 360° rotation = 1500 mm diameter; 180° = 1500 mm × 1200 mm; explicitly uses M.R. (módulo de referência) 800 × 1200 mm. SBi-anvisning 230/BR18 (DK): three-tier A/B/C quality levels; C = code minimum (BR18); B = comfort/best practice for WC users; A = care/residential level. ESKEH (FI): Invalidiliitto accessibility audit framework; pyörätuoli kääntöpaikka 1500 mm minimum; sähköpyörätuoli 2500 mm outdoor turning recommended. CEUD Building for Everyone (IE): NDA/CEUD series; Building Regulations Part M 2022 (latest includes Changing Places). NZS 4121:2001 (NZ): 1500 mm diameter manoeuvring circle; G3/AS1 acceptable solution.
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:24:**Opus 4.6 synthesis note (2026-03-29):** Synthesis reviewed. This is the most evidence-rich BPC in the compendium (20+ key sources, 24 jurisdictions, Tier 1 IDeA Center anthropometric research). Five findings. (1) The 1524 mm ADA rejection is correct and well-evidenced (IDeA Center, Summer Foundation/UTS, Flemmer 2025 NZ). The guidebook’s tier-ladder (1500 code / 1700 best practice / 1800 power WC) is the most defensible specification structure across all reviewed BPCs. Cross-reference bathroom BPC Opus synthesis: the bathroom turning circle inherits this tier-ladder (1500 standard / 1800 power WC). (2) The grab bar type divergence (ADA horizontal vs evidence-based vertical/bilateral) is correctly identified as unresolved. The bathroom BPC Opus synthesis provides the resolution: Kennedy et al. 2015 (Tier 1) demonstrates vertical bars produce smallest COP deviation; bilateral provision serves both transfer directions. The guidebook should specify both horizontal AND vertical as the combined best practice, not choose one. (3) The IT three-tier framework (accessibility/visitability/adaptability) and DK three-quality-level framework (A/B/C) are structurally analogous to the guidebook’s own Universal Mode/1/2 hierarchy. This convergence should be explicitly noted in Part 1 §1.4 as international precedent. (4) The Clemson et al. 2023 Cochrane review (38% fall reduction from OT-delivered home hazard reduction) is the strongest single piece of evidence supporting the guidebook’s core premise that OT involvement in built environment design produces measurably better outcomes. This should be prominently cited in Part 1 §1.3 and Part 9 §9.2. (5) No cross-population conflict identified for MOB provisions. MOB turning circles, ramp gradients, and door widths are beneficial or neutral for all other populations. MOB is the only population code with zero conflict flags across all synthesised BPCs. Evidence confidence: HIGH (Tier 1 anthropometric data, Tier 3 Cochrane systematic reviews, 24-jurisdiction convergence on core provisions).
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:34:| Door clear opening: 900 mm best practice; 850 mm minimum | EN, DE, NL | CA/CSA, UK/BS8300, DE/DIN18040 | Tier 5–6 |
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:53:| Turning circle upgrade: conservative (1500mm) vs. best practice (1800mm) | Most statutory codes (1500mm) | VA/PVA/RHFAC/IDeA (1700–1830mm) | Regulatory lag — 1970s anthropometric data not yet superseded in most codes |
references/bpc/population-general/mobility-built-environment.md:116:**Key finding — SG addendum:** BCA Code 2025 (eff. references/bpc/sensory-environment/room-acoustic-performance.md:20:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/sensory-environment/room-acoustic-performance.md:25:Best-practice provision: RT60 ≤ 0.3 s in classrooms ≤ 283 m³. This requirement is now peer-reviewed (Iglehart, 2020, AJA) and formally encoded in ANSI/ASA S12.60-2010/Part 1 Footnote e. It applies to children with hearing aids or cochlear implants. The standard general-population requirement (0.6 s) is demonstrably insufficient for hearing device users; speech perception scores are significantly better at 0.3 s than 0.6 s for this population. Background noise target: ≤ 30–35 dB(A) (more stringent than the standard ≤ 35 dB(A)).
references/bpc/sensory-environment/room-acoustic-performance.md:28:Best-practice provision: No internationally agreed quantified RT60 target specific to autistic users. Bettarello et al. (2021, *Applied Sciences*) and Caniato et al. (2024) confirm that existing standards (ANSI/ASA S12.60, BB93, UNI 11532-2) are calibrated to neurotypical populations and are insufficient for NDV/AUT. Autistic users are significantly more affected by modest background noise increases (52→55 dB(A)) and by sudden noise events than neurotypical users. Evidence supports: lower background noise targets (≤ 35 dB(A); aspiration: ≤ 30 dB(A)), broadband absorption to suppress flutter echo and reflective peaks, no sound masking systems (A-13 doctrine), and sensory gradient zoning (F-01). Sound masking is contraindicated for NDV/AUT.
references/bpc/sensory-environment/room-acoustic-performance.md:31:Best-practice provision: Acoustic calm as primary therapeutic intervention. BrainXchange (Canada, Tier 2 Co-1) documents background noise management as clinically significant for dementia care. Devos et al. (2019, PMC) confirm RT60 measurement and acoustic intervention as standard practice in Belgian nursing homes, with measurable outcomes. Lyngby-Taarbæk case study (Part IX-06) documents 40% reduction in agitation events following acoustic ceiling installation. Targets consistent with NDV: NC-25 (≤ 35 dB(A)), RT60 ≤ 0.5 s in occupied common areas.
references/bpc/sensory-environment/room-acoustic-performance.md:34:Best-practice provision: Flicker-free, low background noise, NC-25 or better. No specific RT60 target for PCS confirmed beyond general accessibility guidance. The PCS population is absent from all room acoustic standards reviewed in any language.
references/bpc/sensory-environment/school-environment-autism.md:13:### Best-practice synthesis
references/bpc/sensory-environment/sensory-processing-model-design-application.md:24:**Opus 4.6 synthesis note (2026-03-29):** This BPC is a framework entry, not a specification entry. Opus review confirms it as the governing theoretical framework for all F-series and sensory-related item specifications. Three findings. (1) The four-profile model (seeking/avoiding/sensitivity/low-registration) correctly identifies that no single environmental setting serves all NDV users. The Detectable Gradient Protocol (three-zone spatial model) is the primary resolution. Individual user control is the secondary resolution. These two mechanisms together provide Tier 1 and Tier 2 coverage. (2) The Allen CDM integration note is critical for DEM+NDV co-occurrence: CDM addresses progressive cognitive decline; Dunn’s model addresses static sensory variation. In facilities serving both populations (aged care with NDV residents), specifications must satisfy both frameworks simultaneously. This is a design constraint, not a conflict — CDM-simplified environments (low complexity, consistent layout) are compatible with sensory-avoiding/sensory-sensitivity provisions. The conflict arises only with sensory-seeking provisions in dementia settings, where stimulating environments may increase agitation. Route to cross-population-conflict-mapper. (3) PAS 6463:2022 is correctly identified as the sole national standard operationalising this framework. No other jurisdiction has equivalent regulation. This means all NDV sensory specifications in the guidebook exceed any mandatory requirement — they are Tier 4–5 (beyond-code best practice). Evidence confidence: HIGH for the theoretical framework (Dunn 1997 is Tier 1 OT clinical research with extensive replication); LOW for specific built-environment applications of the framework (no RCT validating specific design thresholds derived from the model).

references/connections/bathrooms-and-wet-areas/connections.md:295:**Connection:** MOB BPC: ADA 1524mm turning circle rejected; 1800mm best practice; CSA B651 2100mm for 95th-percentile power chairs. DEM BPC: toilet visible from bedroom doorway; loop circulation; no dead-ends. references/fdr/fold-down-grab-bar-specification.md:50:**Clinical reasoning:** For unilateral UPL the design problem is laterality-dependent in a way it is not for bilateral users. The fold-down bar position must be specified relative to the user's functional side, not as a population-average bilateral provision. This is the OT reasoning: assess which side is functional → specify bar on that side. For a bilateral household, a fold-down on each side (one deployed for the current user, both available for future occupants) is the DAR best practice.

references/methodology-evidence-hierarchy-mapping.md:33:| Tier 6 | Statutory codes | Legally enforceable accessibility codes. **Code-baseline citations only** — the regulatory floor, never a best-practice anchor |
references/methodology-evidence-hierarchy-mapping.md:39:- **Tiers 4–6 are the regulatory stratum,** walled off from best-practice anchoring: convergence of standards/codes is convergence-not-evidence (`tier-system.md` §3). A T4/T5 source can support a best-practice claim only where its own evidence basis is documented as traceable to T1/T2 (re-graining rule, `governance/evidence-architecture.md` §4 G1, PROPOSED).
references/methodology-evidence-hierarchy-mapping.md:58:| Tiers 4–6 | Not classifiable | Not classifiable | GRADE does not rate consensus standards; the project classifies them explicitly — as a regulatory stratum, not as best-practice evidence |
references/methodology-evidence-hierarchy-mapping.md:165:v1's two-marker system is superseded: it allowed "Tier 4–6 only → ●" with the rationale that "code compliance is inherently evidence-based (the evidence being the code itself)." That rationale is the convergence-laundering failure mode `tier-system.md` §3 names, in its oldest recorded form. Under v2, a T4–6-only basis renders ◐/○ and — per the PROPOSED `governance/evidence-architecture.md` G1 (pending owner ratification) — would be flagged `regulatory_stratum_only` in the determination table and carry the regulatory-stratum register language in every audience rendering, never best-practice language (invariant I3).

references/project-standards.md:17:RULE: Evidence hierarchy is seven tiers, per governance/tier-system.md (OPERATIVE 2026-05-25, owner directive "t2>t3 this is enshrined"; DR-2026-05-29 ACCEPTED 2026-07-13). Tier 1 = primary research with intervention-level or biomechanical control (OT-prioritized, not OT-exclusive, per D-E); Co-1 = lived experience / participatory design (CRPD Art. 4.3, co-primary with Tier 1); Tier 2 = synthesis — (a) systematic reviews / meta-analyses AND (b) named-organisation evidence-based standards; Co-2 = OT professional body CPGs (co-primary with Tier 2); Tier 3 = lower-control primary clinical research + grey-literature primary research (supporting; rarely the sole basis — T3-alone never reaches `stated`, per the tier3-stated-threshold DR); Tiers 4–6 = the regulatory stratum (international standards, national frameworks, statutory codes) — walled off from best-practice anchoring; convergence-not-evidence. The evidence hierarchy determines what constitutes best practice. Where higher-tier evidence exists, it governs.
references/project-standards.md:20:RULE: Best practice means the most accommodating, thoughtful, respectful, dignified, and usable condition achievable for the disabled person. Best practice is determined by the evidence hierarchy — Tier 1 primary research, Co-1 lived experience, Tier 2 synthesis (SR/MA + named-organisation evidence-based standards), Co-2 OT CPGs — not by code consensus. Codes are Tier 6: the compliance floor, not the aspiration. Code consensus across jurisdictions establishes what most codes agree on; it does not establish what is most accommodating or most dignified. A BPC best_practice_synthesis that derives its value solely from code consensus is a weak-band (○) best-practice claim — "best practice as currently known," valid only when flagged code-derived with the convergence-not-evidence caveat, and never rendered unflagged or above the weak band (amended 2026-07-21, owner directive, Option A; `decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md`). An unflagged or full-strength code-consensus best-practice claim is in error. The test: does this specification provide the most accommodating, dignified, usable condition the evidence supports — or does it merely reflect what codes agree on?

references/project-standards.md:26:RULE: Evidence specification markers, per governance/tier-system.md §5. ● = confirmed evidence base (T1, T2 both streams, T3-clinical, Co-1, Co-2). ◐ = policy/standards basis only, not primary evidence (T4, T5). ○ = grey, expert consensus, thin base, unconfirmed (T3-grey, [EXPERT CONSENSUS], [THIN BASE], T6). A T4–6-only basis never renders best-practice language in any register (G1b, invariant I3). Every specification sentence carries a marker; for quantitative specifications the confirmed marker additionally requires value-level support, not only direction. Unmarked = error.

references/project-standards.md:172:RULE: Specification language is tier-appropriate and does not claim authority the project does not have. The guidebook is an advocacy project (see Core Doctrine), not a standard-setting body. Specification constructions by mode: Universal Mode — "Code requires [value] per [standard]" (authority is the code, not the guidebook). Population Mode — "[Evidence tier] evidence supports [value]" or "Best practice based on [N] jurisdictions and [tier] evidence is [value]" (authority is the evidence). Person Mode — "OT assessment determines position within [range] based on [functional parameter]" (authority is the OT). The former convention of blanket "shall be" constructions is retired — "shall be" implies mandating authority the project does not possess. Always run prose-style-checker before vol2-item-formatter. Existing Part 4 text using "shall be" requires a voice pass (A4 or C-stage find-and-replace).

references/project-standards.md:606:RULE: Model routing names a CLASS, never a version. All model-routing text — decision records, skill headers, workplan phase tables, governance prose — names a model class (`opus`, `fable`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, `human`) and never a version number. Version numbers are stripped on touch. Rationale: a version pin dates the moment it is written, and a rule pinned to a retired number reads as excluding the model that replaced it — which is how a routing FLOOR silently becomes a routing CEILING. This restates and completes DR-2026-06-10-synthesis-model-floor, which ratified rule #2 as a capability floor ("Opus-class or above") and whose §4 owed a caller sweep it did not perform. Applied 2026-07-25: 80 version pins stripped across 43 files in `skills/`; `fable` added as a synthesis-capable tier in `governance/decision-protocol.md` §4.2/§4.4 and `schemas/decision.py` (`^(opus|fable|sonnet|haiku|human|legacy)/…`); the "Opus 4.6"/"Sonnet 4.6" examples in §4.2 corrected to class names. `fable` and `opus` satisfy the `best_practice_synthesis` floor; `sonnet` and `haiku` do not. Paired decision: DR-2026-07-25-rendered-document-integrity-gate §3.4.

references/project-standards.md:642:RULE: Adversarial review is a truth-instrument bound to evidence, never a plan-instrument. An adversarial pass may be commissioned ONLY against a diff that (a) wrote rows to the research tables (`search_executions`, `search_candidates`, `evidence_sources`, `source_slug_links`, `evidence_population_match`, `source_value_extractions`, `specifications`, `convergence_assessment`, `jurisdictional_values`, `case_studies`, `economics_entries`) or (b) authored or amended a synthesis artifact (`references/bpc-reasoning/*`, `best_practice_synthesis`, a rendered determination page). Plans, critiques, censuses, handoffs, registers, session records, Decision Records and this ledger are not adversarial-pass subjects; they receive the mechanical gates already registered and nothing more. The rule was earned by measurement: **at least ten fresh-context adversarial passes ran between 2026-07-14 and 2026-08-19, and not one examined research output**, because there is none — every research table stands at zero rows. The fresh-session independence convention (`workplan/2026-08-15-adversarial-brief-pr103.md` §0, "Run this in fresh context. Do not read the authoring session's reasoning first") is epistemically correct AND is the mechanism guaranteeing a new session per critique, so critique → plan → critique became the tightest loop in the repository. The one counter-example is the standard to copy: `sessions/session_2026-07-26-energy-conservation-rest-points-seating-adversarial.md`, the only pass ever run on research output, produced **seven sustained findings in a single session**, all against the author's own admitted batch, catching error in both directions — a T1-candidate under-tiered at T3 and a no-participants discussion piece over-tiered, plus one dataset admitted twice under two ref_ids and a circular value. Its own diagnosis is the thing this rule protects: *"a claim pitched at a confidence the retrieval didn't earn."* **The agonist needs no construction: R1-R15 already compel the authoring session to file its complete affirmative case as rows.** The antagonist attacks that recorded case through named lenses — existence, fidelity, independence, tier, population, contrary-finding, recognition by the population served, and query-shape — and the cheapest agonist-antagonist mechanic is blind-then-compare: re-grade tier and population-match independently BEFORE seeing the author's grades, then diff. A pass whose subject is a plan cannot be wrong about anything a reader could check.

references/project-standards.md:4853:1. **Best practice is the most accommodating value found, not the commonest.** The value a determination names as best practice is the gentlest or most accommodating one found in practice, in the direction recorded for the parameter (`base_parameters.accessibility_direction`; for ramp gradient, lower is better), unless a study or a professional body improves on it. It is not the modal or the agreed value, and the steepest value a code allows is not highlighted. No study is needed for a value to be named, and a T4 to T6 value is accepted as a baseline without tracing its history. references/project-standards.md:6676:**On convergence (statements 1 to 3).** [ASSUMPTION: the owner's meaning of convergence is descriptive: values coming together, whatever the sources, as in the thirteenth set's answer 1 ("convergence is possible too, and it helps define most common practice (which isn't same as best practice)") — basis: "convergence just means values coming together", and "why wouldn't that make sense" answers a report that the word had been withheld from agreement among codes; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: the written definition of `convergent` in `governance/evidence-methodology.md` §3.2 joins two facts in one word: whether values come together (descriptive), and whether the agreement is corroboration by independent evidence (a confidence claim: "The converged claim carries higher confidence than either dimension alone", against the doctrine `grep -n -F 'precedent counts documents; evidence counts roots' governance/evidence-architecture.md`, under which agreeing codes are one fact about transmission, not many facts). The owner's reading separates them: convergence is the first; the second is the independent-roots count, shown beside every convergence statement and never implied by the word — basis: the first statement defines the word by the first fact only, and H1 already holds the second as a count of roots; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: therefore agreement among codes alone (Tier 4 to 6) is convergence, and it is most common practice, shown with its source count and its independent-roots count; the T-068 carve-out that left it `pending_assessment` ("agreement among regulatory sources only; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: convergence stays descriptive and its significance is weighted by the tiers of the converging sources: agreement among Tier 1 items is more significant than agreement among Tier 4 items — basis: "different tiers of evidence have different importance, so tier 1 item convergence is more significant than tier 4 item convergence"; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: convergence, as a status or as any output of the convergence assessment, is not an input to selecting the best-practice value or to strengthening its marker: best practice is selected by the specification pass as the most accommodating value in the parameter's recorded direction (the rulings of 2026-10-30, `grep -n -F 'best practice =/= most common or agreed upon' references/project-standards.md`; unratified.]

references/project-standards.md:6690:**What the statements do.** (000) Bound words land as the comparator their direction names (the reading above), written in the extraction guidance, with no code. (00) Ranges are included: every figure is grouped as the interval it states, points being the special case, and no figure is reduced to a point. (0) Statement 6 sets the working tolerance for cluster membership at 5%, in the reading above. (1) OD-43's answer line of the thirteenth set is **refined, not withdrawn**: `OD-43: agreement between any two kinds of evidence counts as convergence; convergence defines most common practice, which is not best practice` stands, and its reach widens to agreement among any sources, codes alone included, and across any tiers (a Tier 1 and a Tier 6 source). No new status line is written: OD-43's latest status line already reads ANSWERED BY THE OWNER, and the answer line is the one this entry refines. (2) The thirteenth set's "What answer 1 does not decide", item (1) (whether agreement among regulatory sources alone is convergence) is decided: it is. Its item (2) (tolerance) is answered by statement 6, as the owner's working view; its item (3) (how most common practice is shown on a page) stays open. (3) The integrator's "conservative choice" in T-068 (agreement among codes alone kept `pending_assessment`) and, to the same extent, the delegated OD-43 resolution of 2026-11-13 are overtaken on contact; the delegated entry stays as history. (4) Every convergent statement carries, beside the word: the number of sources, the number of independent roots (counted as H1 counts them), and each converging source with its tier and weight band, so a code-only convergence reads as one root where the codes share one (or none, where their figure traces to no measured root), and a cross-tier convergence shows each tier. A convergence among codes alone is labelled most common practice and carries the code-derived flag the ledger already requires for regulatory-only bases (`regulatory_stratum_only`, with the convergence-not-evidence caveat); it is never labelled best practice and never raises confidence. (5) The status `convergent` no longer asserts higher confidence; whatever confidence statement is made attaches to the roots count and the tiers listed, not to the word. (6) A convergent assessment changes neither which value is named best practice nor the determination's marker (●, ◐, ○). **No code path lets it today**, read 2026-11-14 and to be re-read, not trusted: the engine's marker is the floor mean of the governing sources' bands and never reads the convergence (`grep -n -A14 '^    marker = None' scripts/assess/assess_cell.py`); its state comes from the tiers present, before the convergence row is built; the value comes from the governing claims and the parameter's direction; the only views that read `convergence_assessment` are `v_divergence` and `v_convergence_sources`, which feed no state, marker or value (`python3 -I -c "import sqlite3;c=sqlite3.connect('file:data/guidebook.db?mode=ro',uri=True);print(c.execute(\"select type,name from sqlite_master where sql like '%convergence%' and type in ('view','trigger')\").fetchall())"`); and the validator reads the convergence status only to refuse a `stated` cell resting on Tier 3 alone, which lowers a state and never raises one. T-068's test guards it. Two prose callers tie convergence to best practice or to state and are T-068's to sweep: the voice-pattern row for `single_axis` (Tier 4–6 only) (`grep -n -F "jurisdictions' standards converge on [value] — best practice as currently known" governance/evidence-methodology.md`), which joins a code convergence to "best practice" in one sentence (the weak-band value is named best practice as the most accommodating value under Option A, not because the codes agree); and §2.3's richness criteria (`grep -n -F 'converging on the same value or range, establishing code consensus' governance/evidence-methodology.md`), where code convergence decides `provisional` against `pending`, a state of evidence sufficiency rather than a marker or a value, which the engine does not check (`grep -n -F 'value-level convergence unverified' scripts/assess/assess_cell.py`). (7) A parameter whose evidence forms two or more clusters on different values is an investigation cue: the engine records it as an open gap row that someone must disposition, through the existing gaps mechanism and an existing category (no vocabulary is added), naming the clusters by the command that lists them; `v_divergence` and `/batch-done`'s report list such parameters as the investigation queue. workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/RENDER-CONTRACT-parameters-3-and-12.md:30:- **C9. Voice: describe and attribute; do not adjudicate a correct path.** The guidebook's own voice says what a source states, where, and in what state ("the Irish Technical Guidance Document M, 2022, Table 2, states ..."). It does not say a value is correct, safe or adequate, and it uses no "should" or "must" of its own; a source's "should" or "must" is quoted as the source's. **"Best practice" is not banned**: the ratified register map (`governance/evidence-architecture.md` section 6) renders "best practice: X [●]", and the posture DR defines it as the practice the evidence best supports, offered as a figure for thought. It may appear only as that map defines it, with its marker, its derivation and its disagreements in view, never as a bare endorsement. The ledger of 2026-10-30 (item 1 and its second entry) rules that the value named as best practice is the most accommodating one found in the parameter's direction, and that a value resting on T4 to T6 alone keeps the ○ band and the weak, code-derived flag; the phrase stays, under those conditions.

workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/current-state-only.md:3:description: "Owner direction - the guidebook is about best practice NOW; use the current edition of each source and do not research or record history, superseded editions or edition-to-edition changes."
workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/current-state-only.md:11:The project is interested in what best practice is **now**. Use only the current in-force edition of each standard or code. Do not investigate or record how a provision changed over time, and do not read superseded editions.
workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/spec-page-requirements.md:33:**ACCESSIBILITY IS A HARD CONSTRAINT (owner, 2026-10-29):** all site work must comply with web accessibility standards and best practices: WCAG 2.2 AA minimum plus COGA (project standard in governance/armature_v3_review.md). Wins any conflict with aesthetics. Itemised, testable list in audits/spec-page-design-requirements-2026-10-29.md section 6a; never self-certify compliance, record what was actually tested.
workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/spec-page-requirements.md:35:**DEFINITION OF BEST PRACTICE (owner, 2026-10-29, stated repeatedly; supersedes any "Guidebook position/working position"):** the Guidebook takes no positions. Best practice = the best way to do something according to how it benefits the intended group; it is NOT the most common or most agreed value. It is the most accommodating/gentlest value found in any current standard (including preferred values) unless an academic article or professional body improves on it. Never present "most common" or "agreed" values as best practice; never label something a Guidebook position or recommendation. Judge per item and per population: the question is always who benefits and how much. Legal maximums are floors for comparison, shown as such.
workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/spec-page-requirements.md:36:**Doctrines to honour on every spec page (read 2026-10-29):** governance/mission-and-epistemics.md (CANONICAL: thinking tool, questions-led, not a prescription manual, within-population variability, best practice by evidence hierarchy not code consensus, Co-1 co-primary), references/project-standards.md Core Doctrine (advocacy project not an authority: no "shall be"; every specification carries evidence marker ● evidence-based or ○ inferred; person tier = OT assessment), governance/audience-priority.md (designers and disabled people primary; OTs and policymakers secondary), governance/tier-system.md. Open owner decisions recorded in audits/spec-page-design-requirements-2026-10-29.md section 10. Controls (units, three lenses, filters) must sit ABOVE all information. Do not highlight the steepest slope allowed in the codes.
workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/owner-rules/spec-page-requirements.md:46:- Best practice: the gentlest/most accommodating value found in practice IS best practice; no academic study is required (they are often unavailable). Existence in practice is the evidence. (This differs from the mission doc's "T4-T6 walled off from best-practice anchoring"; the owner's statement needs a doctrine decision record.)

workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:1:# Best-Practices Assessment System — Design
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:5:**Core gap it closes:** the mission defines a per-cell best-practice determination, but `specifications` is **empty (0 rows)** — the determination engine is unbuilt.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:11:Your mission already prescribes *how* best practice is decided. "Best practice is determined by the evidence hierarchy, not code consensus" (doctrine #2), and every `(parameter × population)` cell must carry one of four states (doctrine #4): **`stated`** (≥Tier 3 OR Co‑1 OR Co‑2), **`provisional`**, **`pending`**, **`not_applicable`**. The system's job is to *apply* this consistently, dynamically, and auditably across all cells. It must never let a code-only determination render above **weak-band (○), flagged, "best practice as currently known"** — a Tier‑6-only cell stays `provisional`, never `stated`, and code is the floor (amended 2026-07-21, Option A; `decisions/DR-2026-07-21-evidence-architecture-option-a-execution.md`).
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:38:   - `pending` ⟺ sparse across all dimensions → emit `[BEST-PRACTICE-PENDING]` + link to a `gaps` row.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:45:6. **Falsification condition:** every `stated`/`provisional` cell records *what evidence would overturn it* (defends against the known BPC‑hallucination failure mode; ties to `gaps.falsification_condition`).
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:53:**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-13):** the sketch below is superseded by the actual migration, per `decisions/DR-2026-07-12-evidence-cell-state-schema-reconciliation.md`'s own "Consequences if ratified" section: *"`workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md` §4's SQL sketch is superseded by the actual migration; its phasing plan (§8) is otherwise unaffected."* The live schema (`scripts/migrations/026_reconcile_specifications.sql`, `027_regulatory_stratum_only.sql`) keeps `specifications`'s identity as `(item_code, population_code)` — not `(slug, population, jurisdiction)` as sketched below — and structures the within-population range as `value_min`/`value_max`/`value_unit` rather than a free-text `value_range`. Jurisdiction-specific values live in the separate `jurisdictional_values` table, deliberately not folded into this one (conflating a jurisdiction-agnostic best-practice determination with a jurisdiction-specific code floor is exactly the failure mode this schema exists to prevent — see `governance/tier-system.md` §3). The sketch is left below for the phasing-plan context (§8, unaffected); do not implement against it — implement against the live migrations.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:84:- `v_best_practice` — current `stated`/`provisional` determinations with governing evidence.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:95:The **determination is fixed**; the **emphasis re-ranks** by audience × use-pattern (the mission's dynamic-weighting goal). A small profile table parameterizes a view — it changes *what is foregrounded*, never the 4‑state verdict (code stays the floor for everyone; no weighting profile can manufacture *full-strength* best practice from code — a code-only cell renders only as weak-band ○ flagged best practice, per amended I3, 2026-07-21 Option A):
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:99:| Designer · aspiration/decision-frame | emphasize Tier 1 + Co‑1; show best-practice value *above* code; surface DAR provisions |
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:117:Encode doctrine + the 7-point test as an audit script (`scripts/audit/best_practice_integrity.py`, exit 1 on violation; promote to blocking CI):
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:120:2. Every non-`not_applicable` cell has a determination **or** a `[BEST-PRACTICE-PENDING]`+`gap_id` (doctrine #4 "silence is not the default").
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:144:- **Phase 1 — engine + backfill:** implement the pure determination function; nail the slug→population→evidence join; backfill all `(slug × population)` cells that have evidence; emit `pending`+gap for the rest. This is where the *first real best-practice table* comes into existence.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:145:- **Phase 2 — gates:** `best_practice_integrity.py` audit → blocking CI.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:147:- **Phase 4 — surface:** per-audience "why is this best practice?" view (Datasette/site), showing governing evidence, code-floor delta, within-population range, and divergence synthesis.
workplan/best-practices-assessment-system.md:155:It grounds best practice in the hierarchy not code (#3); surfaces where Co‑1 vs Tier‑1 govern, converge, and diverge (#4); never goes silent on thin evidence (#4 → `pending`); keeps data verifiable and methodology declared (#7 → `governing_refs` + `rule_version` + `falsification`); and teaches rather than dictates by exposing the evidence and the range rather than a single imposed number (#1, #6). Every determination is a reproducible, falsifiable, provenance-bearing artifact — policy-grade by construction.

workplan/next-steps-synthesis-2026-07-14.md:56:All four strategies keep the **same act** on the critical path: *a single, measurably-unreliable operator authoring adjudicated "best-practice" determinations.* They differ only in **volume** (S1: 82; S4: ~10), **sequence** (S3: build the jig first), or **wrapper** (S2: mail a review afterward). None removes the biased synthesis step from the critical path; none adds a standing second author; and all four quietly define review success as *gating-on-attempt*, where a logged non-response clears the gate — i.e. **they have already pre-conceded no second human will engage** (0 of 6 outreach batches sent since 2026-05-02; CI deleted 2026-06-09).
workplan/next-steps-synthesis-2026-07-14.md:66:**S5 — SHIP THE EVIDENCE MAP, NOT THE AUTHORITY.** Publish what already exists and is solo-sound: the 640-source, tier-graded, Co-1/Co-2-classified corpus as a navigable annotated index by item × population × jurisdiction, **surfacing where sources disagree** and declining to emit a single adjudicated "best-practice value." The reader performs the last-mile synthesis. This *subtracts* the adjudication layer where the 67% bias lives (the opposite move from S1–S4); it matches the mission's own language; it converts "permanently solo" from fatal to fine (indexing/grading is what solo+AI has already done to quality); the empty `conflicts` table stops being a blocker because **the disagreement becomes the product**; and it ships in weeks at a fraction of the cost. Honest boundary: tier-assignment is still a judgment, so S5 shrinks the risk surface rather than eliminating it — but it is a far smaller, more checkable surface than per-cell synthesis.
